## 1 · Release decision (Question 1)

**Recommendation: cut the stable release from `main` after merging PR #46 — do not promote rc7.1 as-is, and do not attempt to finish Task-087 before the deadline.**

### 1.1 The pivotal fact: finishing Task-087 would not remove the manual TLS commands anyway

PR #46 intentionally ships the TLS-repair control plane **dark** — `provider_helper_available()` is hardcoded `False` (`webapp/ts_provider_http.py:120`) and `PROVIDER_TLS_REPAIR_BROWSER_MUTATION_ENABLED = false` (`webapp/js/src/setup-wizard.js:17`); no env var or config can flip either, and a unit test pins the gate. Turning the feature on requires the remainder of Gate 3 (an enablement PR + release-owner sign-off) **plus** Gate 4: managed-network package validation on both CPU and CUDA package variants. That cannot land credibly by 07-10, and enabling a brand-new browser→host trust boundary days before handoff, with no pilot soak, would be the wrong risk even if it could.

**Consequence: the User Guidance decision is already made.** Document the manual `repair-provider-tls.cmd` flow (the validated Task-086 baseline — the current guide already references it) and finalize the guides now. Nothing about Task-087 blocks them.

### 1.2 Why main + PR #46 beats rc7.1 as the stable base

The rc7.1 tag was verified during this review to contain two real bugs that are already fixed on the PR #46 branch:

- **F4** — Google key validation classifies a 2xx response with an empty JSON body as `TLS_OK` (`ts_provider_http.py:336` at rc7.1: `if provider == "google" and body_json:` — a falsy `{}` skips the status check). A proxy or gateway returning 200 with a stripped body persists an invalid key as valid. Managed networks are exactly where this bites.
- **F5** — geocoding aborts on a TLS-categorized error instead of falling back to the second configured provider (`ts_geocoding.py:480` at rc7.1). A provider-specific TLS fault silently costs addresses on detections. The fix on PR #46 implements the decided hybrid: fall back, carry a sanitized warning, mark the provider unhealthy per-run.

The rest of PR #46's risk surface is contained: the control plane is inert (see 1.1), all 10 findings from the independent quality review were implemented and re-verified at `533e18b`, and CI green is now meaningful (the pipefail fix means test failures actually fail the build).

### 1.3 Timeline

| When | What |
|---|---|
| 07-08 | Strip CI log-publishing residue from the PR (+ CI hardening: bundle-freshness guard, contract test into `ci.yml`); undraft and squash-merge PR #46 (22 of its 30 commits are CI iterations); merge `cdcai/main` (README resolution validated in a dry run — one conflicted file, 2 hunks); land the pre-tag source pass (namespace `J-Schulein`→`cdcai`, redactions, HANDOFF docs); delete the stale `v0.1.0` tag; tag `v0.1.0`; dispatch `container-publish` (cpu, cuda121); build packages with `package-release.ps1`. |
| 07-08 → 07-09 | Regression validation with the existing replayable harness (`Validation Evidence/rc7.1-docker-qa-2026-07-07/`; ~3.5–5 h wall clock) + targeted Setup-Wizard smoke S1–S7 covering the F4/F5/F7 fixes. Full protocol: Implementation Strategy §D5. |
| 07-09 → 07-10 | Publish the fork release (`prerelease=false`, mark **Latest** — every existing release is a prerelease, so this is the first stable). Rename the assets ZIP for the stable version + regenerate its `.sha256` sidecar (validated byte-level: `<lowercase-hex><2 spaces><filename>` + CRLF). Final User Guidance pass (edit lists in §6.15). |
| 07-11 → 07-13 | Buffer + migration phases (§3). |

**Sequencing rule (from the pass-2 fact-check):** the namespace pass and the seven packaged-doc URL edits must land **before** `git tag v0.1.0` — `package-release.ps1` requires a clean tree at the tag, so post-tag doc fixes would force a v0.1.1. Version strings themselves are fully parameterized (no version-bump edits exist), but the namespace edits are deliberate source changes that belong in the tagged tree.

**Version name:** delete the stale 2025-12-04 `v0.1.0` tag (a "15% complete" milestone that collides with the natural stable name), then reuse **v0.1.0** to match the `v0.1.0-rc*` line. `v0.1.1` is the safe alternative if cached clones are a concern. The release version flows entirely from script parameters — no version-bump source edits exist (the namespace pass in §2 is a separate, deliberate source change).

**Fallback (decision point end of 07-09):** if regression validation fails and cannot be fixed same-day, edit the existing **rc7.1** release — uncheck *prerelease*, mark Latest, add a Known Issues section covering F4/F5 and the manual TLS commands. Guidance needs no change either way. PR #46 still merges to `main` so the fixes hand off in source.

**Task-087 disposition:** hand off as documented future work — keep the task file, add the missing `docs/support/host-helper.md` (2,559 lines of helper PowerShell ship in every package today with zero documentation), and include it in the backlog export.

## 3 · Migration runbook (validated against live GitHub state; commands dry-run in Pass 2)

Verified mechanics: true GitHub fork of cdcai/TowerScout; upstream has **zero** tags, releases, and workflows but a diverged main (+2 README commits); no git-LFS; no custom Actions secrets (only `GITHUB_TOKEN`); `container-publish.yml` auto-adapts its image name to the owning repo. GitHub Releases, Actions history, issues, and wiki content do **not** move with a git push.

### Phase 0 — Prepare the fork *(JS, by 07-09)*
- Critical actions 1–6 (§2): rotation, branch deletions, CI residue, stale tag, gh auth, stable release published on the fork.
- Commit the namespace pass (action 9) and doc set (action 10).
- `git remote add cdcai https://github.com/cdcai/TowerScout.git && git fetch cdcai && git merge cdcai/main` — resolve README.md preserving the owner's TowerScout Enterprise links → `git push origin main`. Main becomes a fast-forward for cdcai.

### Phase 1 — Owner grants access *(CE)*
- Collaborator write; Issues enabled; Actions/GHCR org policy confirmed (§2 action 11). **Pass-2 dry run proved no credential on this machine can push to cdcai today** (`git push --dry-run` → 403 "Permission … denied to Jschulein") — write access is a hard critical-path dependency; request it 07-08.
- If write access is refused → fallback: PR `J-Schulein:main → cdcai:main` with **written instruction to use "Create a merge commit" — never "Squash and merge"** (squash orphans every tag and breaks the AGPL SOURCE.txt SHA promise in shipped packages).

### Phase 2 — Push history *(JS)*
- `git push cdcai main` (fast-forward after Phase 0).
- Selective tags only: `git push cdcai v0.1.0 v0.1.0-rc7.1` (+ optionally plain `rc1…rc7` tags, no releases). Leave behind: `tls-validation-*` ×3, `gpu-validation-*`, `rc5-candidate.3`, local-only `uat-local-fix-*`.

### Phase 3 — Images & release in cdcai *(JS + CE)*
- Publish images under cdcai. **Pass-2 correction (dry-run verified): `docker pull` → `tag` → `push` does NOT preserve the pinned digest** — the published images are OCI image indexes with buildx provenance attestations, and a docker re-push creates a different digest, 404-ing the digest baked into the packages. Use `regctl image copy` (single-binary download; preserves the index byte-for-byte) or re-dispatch `container-publish.yml` in cdcai and **repackage with the new digests**. crane/regctl/skopeo are not currently installed on this machine.
- **[CE]** set `ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout` package visibility to Public (GHCR defaults to private on first publish).
- Fresh clone from cdcai → rebuild package with `-Image ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout@<digest>` → `gh release create <stable> --repo cdcai/TowerScout …` with the code ZIPs, sidecars, and **one** copy of the 800 MB assets ZIP (byte-identical across all 13 fork releases, sha256 `00599cc4…`), renamed for the stable version with a regenerated sidecar.

### Phase 4 — Verify *(JS)*
- Fresh cdcai clone: `git describe --tags` = stable; `git cat-file -t <SOURCE.txt SHA from a shipped package>` succeeds (AGPL traceability intact).
- Unzip cdcai-built ZIP vs fork stable ZIP; hash-compare file-by-file — expect diffs *only* in manifest/IMAGE.txt/SOURCE.txt/SHA256SUMS lines carrying image ref + timestamps.
- Anonymous `docker pull ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout@<digest>` from a logged-out session; verify release-asset sidecars; one `setup-towerscout.cmd` Docker-CPU smoke on a clean, space-free path.

### Phase 5 — Close out *(JS + CE)*
- Fork README banner → "development moved to cdcai/TowerScout"; **keep the fork alive** until Phase 4 passes (it holds PR/Actions history and the rc-release archive).
- Backlog export to cdcai Issues; owner optionally protects main.
- Disclose at handoff: a rotated Azure key (rotated 2026-07-08) exists in deep history (rewrite impractical — it would invalidate every tag/release; the rotation neutralizes it).

## 6 · All findings with verdicts (pass 1: 100 findings, 1 refuted; pass 2: 51 findings, 0 refuted)

Every pass-1 finding was independently re-checked by a second agent instructed to refute it against the repo and live GitHub API. Pass-2 findings in areas 6.14-6.18 got the same treatment; 6.13 and 6.19 are themselves verification passes (marked SELF-VERIFIED). Verdicts: **CONFIRMED** = reproduced and materially correct · **ADJUSTED** = kernel of truth, claim/severity corrected in the note · **REFUTED** = wrong or moot.

### 6.1 Secrets & sensitive material

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Secrets sweep of working tree + full git history (all 20 remote branches, 4 local branches, all tags; refs enumerated via for-each-ref, stash empty). ONE HIGH finding: a real 86-char Azure Maps subscription key (prefix '7G19') committed 2026-01-05 in .agent_work/tasks/TASK-024-azure-maps-frontend.md — absent from HEAD/main/rc7.1 tips but still browsable TODAY at the tips of public branches 'improvements' and 'feature/geocoding-system-integration' (confirmed via GitHub API) and reachable in the deep history of every branch and tag including origin/main (removal commit 3417c64's parent c2aa6b2 is a main ancestor). Mandatory pre-handoff action: rotate the Azure key and delete the two stale branches. Everything else came back clean: no GitHub tokens (ghp_/github_pat_), no AWS AKIA, no private keys or certificates anywhere in tracked files or history (git log --all on *.pem/*.cer/*.crt/*.pfx/*.p12 → empty; PEM-header grep hits only PowerShell code in scripts/import-tls-ca.ps1); every tracked/historical AIza match is a documented dummy (AIzaSyDEXAMPLE…/AIzaSyEXAMPLE…/AIzaSySecret in tests); no real .env or apikey.txt ever committed (only placeholder .env.example files — the 'security: migrate API keys' commit 0d08728 removed a file-read of apikey.txt, not hardcoded keys); the old leaked Google key from the March 2026 BFG cleanup was rotated and only its 15-char prefix survives in docs/logs (full key verified absent via pickaxe); webapp/tmp.zip in upstream 2024 history is a harmless 297-byte PyTorch CUDA test script; Flask SECRET_KEY is runtime-generated and persisted to gitignored webapp/config/.env (webapp/ts_config.py:567); .gitignore properly covers .env family, apikey.txt, .towerscout-runtime/, towerscout.pem, and hosting/connect-tc.sh; dist/ has zero tracked files; docs/, TowerScoutSite/ and webapp/js contain no embedded keys (password-pattern hits are vendor library code only); untracked .env.uat-backup-20260615 contains no secrets (config vars only, REDACTED-live-keys: NO) but is not gitignored, and untracked .agent_work/evidence/ holds one benign runtime-versions JSON. Low/info items: delete-or-ignore the untracked env backup before final cleanup, stale-branch deletion doubles as cleanup for leftover pre-BFG log blobs, and legacy upstream hosting scripts reference a dead 2021 EC2 host (pem never committed).

#### `secrets-1` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Real Azure Maps subscription key committed to git history AND still browsable at the tips of two public branches on the fork

- **Location:** .agent_work/tasks/TASK-024-azure-maps-frontend.md (and its active/completed variants) — public branches 'improvements' and 'feature/geocoding-system-integration' on https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout, plus origin/main ancestry (commits 7fce732, 6a3ea3d, 42fff14; removed at 3417c64 2026-04-15)
- **Evidence:** An 86-character high-entropy value (prefix '7G19', mixed base64url charset — NOT a placeholder; sits alongside 'your_azure_maps_...' placeholders in the same docs) is assigned to `AZURE_MAPS_SUBSCRIPTION_KEY`. Tip exposure on `improvements` and `feature/geocoding-system-integration` and ancestry from the pre-removal `main` history were independently verified. Context in the doc presents it as the working 'Final Solution' config, not an example. The full key value was never printed during this review; key liveness was not tested.
- **Recommendation:** Before handoff: (1) rotate/regenerate the Azure Maps subscription key in the Azure portal (mandatory regardless of any cleanup, since it has been public); (2) delete the stale public branches 'improvements' and 'feature/geocoding-system-integration' from the fork so the key is no longer at any browsable tip; (3) disclose to cdcai at handoff that a rotated Azure key exists in deep main history, and decide jointly whether they want a rewrite on their side. Do NOT hand off without rotation.
- **Migration relevance:** cdcai will receive this history and public branches; GitHub secret scanning and any reviewer will find it. The key has been publicly exposed since 2026-01-05 (~6 months). A history rewrite of main is impractical before the 2026-07-15 handoff (every tag/release would be invalidated), so rotation is the mandatory remediation.
- **Verifier's note:** Fully reproduced. Extracted the 86-char value (prefix '7G19') from commit 1c51c7a and confirmed: (1) it is at the tips of both public branches — origin/improvements (.agent_work/tasks/completed/TASK-024-azure-maps-frontend.md) and origin/feature/geocoding-system-integration (TASK-024 doc plus active/ variant) — and GitHub API lists both branches on the public fork; (2) c2aa6b2 (key in-tree) is an ancestor of origin/main, commit 3417c64 (2026-04-15) removed it (2 '-' lines in diff), first add 7fce732 dated 2026-01-05; (3) 37/40 refs including all tags v0.1.0-rc1..rc7.1, pr-46, and main contain c2aa6b2 (the only exceptions are the two stale branches — which expose the key directly at their tips — and pre-fork upstream tag v0.1.0); (4) the key is definitively real, not a placeholder: the doc presents it as the tested 'Final Solution' with 'Search API: HTTP 200' verification, and the value carries the genuine modern Azure API key signature format ('...JQQJ99...' with region code). Key is absent from origin/main tip, HEAD 533e18b, and the working tree. One immaterial evidence nit: no 'your_azure_maps_...' placeholder exists in that same doc version. High severity is justified — a real cloud credential browsable at two public branch tips on a fork about to be handed to cdcai; rotation before handoff is mandatory.

### 6.2 Migration mechanics

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Validated the concrete mechanics of migrating J-Schulein/TowerScout back to cdcai/TowerScout using the public GitHub API and read-only git. Overall the migration is mechanically simpler than feared: no git-LFS (nothing in .gitattributes, `git lfs ls-files` empty), no custom Actions secrets (all three workflows use only the automatic secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN), the container-publish CI workflow is already repo-agnostic (`ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}`), cdcai/TowerScout has a single unprotected `main` branch, zero tags, zero releases, zero open PRs, and issues disabled — a nearly blank target. The pinned runtime image digest (ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout@sha256:d686d855...) is confirmed publicly pullable (anonymous GHCR token GET returns 200). However, four things need action before/at handoff: (1) cdcai main has DIVERGED — it carries 2 README-only commits (Chris Edens, 2026-02-11, 'TowerScout Enterprise' links) not present in the fork, so a plain push is not fast-forward and must be preceded by a reconciling merge (never a force-push); (2) the runtime container image lives in Jonathan's personal GHCR namespace and is hardcoded as a default in 3 shipped files — and the `latest-cpu` fallback tag doesn't even exist on GHCR (404); (3) GitHub Releases (13 pre-releases, ~800.9 MB assets each) do not migrate and gh CLI on this machine is installed (2.92.0) but NOT authenticated; (4) a stale `v0.1.0` tag already exists on origin pointing at a 2025-12-04 commit, making blind `git push --tags` and a future v0.1.0 stable release hazardous. Recommended primary path: owner grants Jonathan collaborator write, merge cdcai/main into fork main, then direct `git push cdcai main` + selective tag push (full 8-month history and AGPL source-ref traceability preserved). Fallback: a merge-commit (never squash) PR from fork to cdcai. Options (c) repo transfer and (d) fork detach are not applicable/needed. Checked and fine: SOURCE.txt/release-manifest embed only a bare commit SHA (no repo URL), root release-manifest.v1.json is an empty template filled at package time, bootstrap/setup/compose files contain no raw.githubusercontent or J-Schulein clone URLs beyond the GHCR image refs, .env and dist/ are gitignored.

#### `migration-1` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — cdcai/TowerScout main has diverged from the fork: 2 README commits (Feb 2026) mean the handoff push cannot be a plain fast-forward

- **Location:** https://api.github.com/repos/cdcai/TowerScout/compare/main...J-Schulein:TowerScout:main
- **Evidence:** Compare API: {"status": "diverged", "ahead_by": 98, "behind_by": 2}. Reverse compare identifies the 2 cdcai-only commits: f828b34cda8f and 3e2c65de3c17, both 2026-02-11, author Chris Edens, messages 'Update README.md' ('Added links to TowerScout Enterprise and removed direct links to outside Google Dr...'); files touched: README.md only (+6 -4). Merge base c3ac0ec239a0 (2025-06-18 'Merge pull request #1 from suever/suever/google-updates') exists in the local clone, but cdcai HEAD does not: `git cat-file -t 3e2c65de...` -> 'fatal: could not get object info'. cdcai pushed_at=2026-02-11T18:23:27Z, so it is NOT frozen at the Nov-2025 fork point. cdcai branches API lists exactly one branch: main @ 3e2c65de, "protected": false.
- **Recommendation:** Before handoff: `git remote add cdcai https://github.com/cdcai/TowerScout.git && git fetch cdcai && git merge cdcai/main` on the fork's main, resolving README.md so Chris Edens' 'TowerScout Enterprise' links are preserved; push the merge to origin main first, then to cdcai. Never force-push to cdcai.
- **Migration relevance:** Any `git push cdcai main` will be rejected as non-fast-forward until reconciled. The tempting workaround (--force) would destroy the upstream owner's own Feb-2026 README commits — a bad look for a handoff. Discovering this at push time on 2026-07-13 would burn deadline hours.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced end-to-end: compare API status=diverged ahead_by=98 behind_by=2; reverse compare lists exactly f828b34cda8f and 3e2c65de3c17 (2026-02-11, Chris Edens, 'Update README.md', README.md only +6/-4); merge base c3ac0ec exists in local clone while `git cat-file -t 3e2c65de...` fails; cdcai pushed_at=2026-02-11T18:23:27Z with a single unprotected main branch @ 3e2c65de. Additionally verified the fork ALSO rewrote README.md since the merge base (git diff c3ac0ec..229f59f -- README.md: +82/-22), so both the direct-push and PR routes require an actual README conflict resolution — strengthening the finding. High severity justified: the push to cdcai IS the handoff deliverable and the naive --force workaround would destroy the owner's commits.

#### `migration-2` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Recommended migration path: reconciling merge + direct push with full history (needs collaborator write); fallback merge-commit PR; squash merge must be explicitly ruled out

- **Location:** https://github.com/cdcai/TowerScout (target); local repo c:/Users/Jonat/Documents/TowerScout/TowerScout
- **Evidence:** Option analysis grounded in API/repo state: (a) Giant PR fork->cdcai: possible despite divergence; preserves all 98+ commits ONLY if merged with 'Create a merge commit' — cdcai's allowed merge methods are invisible unauthenticated (API returns allow_squash_merge/allow_merge_commit/allow_rebase_merge = null), so squash risk is real. A squash would orphan all 15 local tags (git tag -l shows v0.1.0-rc1..rc7.1, gpu-validation-2026-06-16, tls-validation-*) AND break AGPL traceability: scripts/package-release.ps1 lines 94-98 embed `git rev-parse HEAD` as source_ref, and the generated SOURCE.txt (lines 415-435) promises 'matching corresponding source available for the exact release ref' — a squashed cdcai history would not contain the SHAs printed in already-shipped rc packages. (b) Direct push after reconcile: exact history + tags, 6 commands, no force needed after migration-1's merge; requires cdcai owner to add Jonathan as collaborator (or run the pushes). (c) GitHub 'Transfer repository': NOT applicable — cdcai/TowerScout (repo id 630905125, created 2023) already exists; transfer cannot overwrite an existing name and would sacrifice cdcai's repo continuity and its 2 Feb-2026 commits. (d) Detach fork via GitHub Support: irrelevant — fork metadata does not block pushing; only worth requesting later if J-Schulein/TowerScout is to be deleted while keeping cdcai independent (they already are independent repos).
- **Recommendation:** Primary: cdcai owner grants Jonathan write; Jonathan runs `git fetch cdcai; git merge cdcai/main` (resolve README), `git push origin main`, `git push cdcai main`, then selective tag push (see migration-5). Fallback if write access is refused: open PR J-Schulein:main -> cdcai:main with explicit written instruction to the owner to use 'Create a merge commit' (never 'Squash and merge'), then owner pushes tags themselves or accepts a follow-up tags-only push.
- **Migration relevance:** This is the core handoff decision; choosing squash-merge PR or transfer by default would silently lose 8 months of history, orphan every release tag, and invalidate the AGPL corresponding-source offer in shipped packages.
- **Verifier's note:** All factual underpinnings reproduce: unauthenticated repo API returns allow_squash_merge/allow_merge_commit/allow_rebase_merge = null (merge-method policy invisible, squash risk real); 15 local tags exist (v0.1.0..rc7.1 plus validation tags); scripts/package-release.ps1:94-103 embeds `git rev-parse HEAD` as source ref and lines 415-435 generate SOURCE.txt promising corresponding source 'for the exact release ref' — shipped rc SOURCE.txt SHAs (e.g. 839ab00a referenced in .agent_work evidence) would be unreachable in a squashed cdcai history; cdcai repo id 630905125 created 2023-04-21 confirms transfer-by-name is impossible; J-Schulein repo is fork:true with parent cdcai/TowerScout so the fallback PR route is available. The AGPL-traceability and tag-orphaning consequences of a squash merge are accurately stated. High severity fair: this is the central handoff decision and the failure mode is silent, irreversible history loss.

#### `migration-3` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Runtime container image lives in personal GHCR namespace (ghcr.io/j-schulein) and is hardcoded as the default in 3 shipped files; the 'latest-cpu' fallback tag does not exist on GHCR

- **Location:** compose.yaml:3; scripts/lib/TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117; scripts/package-release.ps1:37
- **Evidence:** compose.yaml:3 `image: ${TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE:-ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:latest-cpu}`; scripts/lib/TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117 `return "ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:latest-cpu"`; scripts/package-release.ps1:37 `$Image = "ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:latest-cpu"`. GHCR tags/list for j-schulein/towerscout contains only rc-suffixed tags (v0.1.0-rc7.1-cpu, v0.1.0-rc7.1-cuda121, tls-validation-*, task-025-*) — NO 'latest-cpu'; anonymous manifest GET for latest-cpu returns 404 while the digest pinned in packages (sha256:d686d8556443...) returns 200 (publicly pullable). CI is already portable: .github/workflows/container-publish.yml:46 computes `image="ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}"` and is workflow_dispatch-triggered with tag + pytorch_flavor inputs, using only secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN (line 39, permissions packages:write). cdcai currently has zero workflows (actions/workflows API: total_count 0) — they arrive with the code push.
- **Recommendation:** Before the final package build: (1) change the 3 defaults to ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout in a pre-handoff commit; (2) after code push, dispatch container-publish in cdcai for the stable tag (cpu + cuda121) — or `crane copy ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout@sha256:d686... ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout:<tag>` to preserve the validated digest; (3) owner must flip the new ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout package visibility to Public (GHCR packages default to private on first publish); (4) rebuild the stable release package with `-Image ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout@<digest>`.
- **Migration relevance:** After handoff, every shipped package and the repo defaults keep pulling from Jonathan's personal account. If that account/package is ever deleted or made private, cdcai's product stops being installable. Also the fallback tag being nonexistent means the compose default only works because packages pin TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE in .env — a latent trap for cdcai maintainers.
- **Verifier's note:** All three file:line claims exact at HEAD 533e18b: compose.yaml:3 `${TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE:-ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:latest-cpu}`, scripts/lib/TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117 hardcoded return, scripts/package-release.ps1:37 default. GHCR tags/list (anonymous token) returns 23 tags for j-schulein/towerscout — no 'latest-cpu'; manifest GET latest-cpu → HTTP 404 while digest sha256:d686d8556443... → HTTP 200 (publicly pullable; digest also matches .agent_work/evidence/TASK-083 records). container-publish.yml is portable as claimed (line 4 workflow_dispatch, line 26 packages:write, line 39 secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN only, line 46 ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}); cdcai actions/workflows total_count=0. Packages do pin TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE via .env rewrite (package-release.ps1:400-404), confirming the nonexistent fallback tag is a latent trap. High severity justified: post-handoff product installability depends on a personal GHCR namespace.

#### `migration-4` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — GitHub Releases and their ~800.9 MB asset sets do not migrate with a push; gh CLI on this machine is installed but not authenticated

- **Location:** https://api.github.com/repos/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases vs https://api.github.com/repos/cdcai/TowerScout/releases
- **Evidence:** J-Schulein has 13 releases, all prerelease=true, each with 4-7 assets totaling ~800.7-800.9 MB (e.g. 'v0.1.0-rc7.1 pre 6 assets 800.9 MB'); cdcai releases API returns [] and tags API returns []. Releases/assets are repo-scoped GitHub objects — `git push` moves none of them. `gh auth status` on this machine: 'You are not logged into any GitHub hosts. To log in, run: gh auth login' (gh version 2.92.0 installed). Largest single asset is well under GitHub's 2 GiB per-asset limit, so re-upload is feasible.
- **Recommendation:** Recreate ONLY the final stable release in cdcai: Jonathan runs `gh auth login` first (flag: currently unauthenticated), then after rebuilding the package from a fresh cdcai clone, `gh release create <stable-tag> --repo cdcai/TowerScout --title "TowerScout <ver>" --notes-file <notes> dist/<package>.zip dist/<package>.zip.sha256 <remaining assets>`. Keep the fork's rc releases as historical archive; add a README note in the fork pointing to cdcai for current releases.
- **Migration relevance:** The handoff deliverable is a stable release package published under cdcai — without recreating the release there, cdcai gets code but no installable product. ~10.4 GB total across all 13 releases makes 'migrate everything' impractical and pointless.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced: J-Schulein/TowerScout has 13 releases, all prerelease=true, 4-7 assets each at 800.7-800.9 MB per release (v0.1.0-rc7.1 = 6 assets, 800.9 MB), ~10.41 GB total, largest single asset 0.8 GB (under the 2 GiB per-asset limit); cdcai releases API returns [] and tags API returns []; `gh auth status` on this machine exits 1 with 'You are not logged into any GitHub hosts' (gh 2.92.0). Releases/assets are indeed repo-scoped and do not move with git push. High severity fair since publishing a stable release under cdcai is half the handoff deliverable; the gh-unauthenticated detail alone is trivial but the substantive gap (cdcai gets code with no installable product unless the release is recreated) is real.

#### `migration-5` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Stale v0.1.0 tag already on origin points at a 2025-12-04 commit — blind `git push cdcai --tags` would publish a wrong/misleading stable tag

- **Location:** refs/tags/v0.1.0 on https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout (annotated tag f00e870c -> commit e78974d6)
- **Evidence:** `git ls-remote --tags origin` shows `f00e870ca525...refs/tags/v0.1.0` and `e78974d6f2fc...refs/tags/v0.1.0^{}`; `git log -1 v0.1.0` -> 'e78974d6 2025-12-04 10:28:24 -0500 chore: cleanup test files and improve gitignore'. That commit predates rc1-rc7.1 validation work by ~7 months. No GitHub Release is attached to it. 15 tags exist locally; 14 on origin (uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 is local-only).
- **Recommendation:** Decide before tagging stable: either delete/re-point v0.1.0 (`git push origin :refs/tags/v0.1.0` then retag the stable commit) or choose a fresh version (e.g. v0.1.1/v1.0.0). Push tags to cdcai SELECTIVELY: `git push cdcai <stable-tag> v0.1.0-rc7.1` — never `--tags`.
- **Migration relevance:** If the stable release is meant to be v0.1.0, the name is already taken by a December commit: `gh release create v0.1.0` would silently attach the release to the wrong commit, and `git push cdcai --tags` would export the trap into cdcai along with 5 throwaway validation tags (tls-validation-*, gpu-validation-*, rc5-candidate.3).
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced: `git ls-remote --tags origin` shows f00e870c → refs/tags/v0.1.0 with peeled e78974d6; `git log -1 v0.1.0` → e78974d6 2025-12-04 'chore: cleanup test files and improve gitignore' (7 months before rc7.1 @ 1152c16f 2026-06-29); no GitHub Release exists for v0.1.0 in the 13-release list; 15 tags local vs 14 on origin with uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 the local-only one; exactly 5 throwaway tags on origin (tls-validation x3, gpu-validation-2026-06-16, v0.1.0-rc5-candidate.3). `gh release create v0.1.0` would attach to the existing stale tag, and `--tags` would export all of it to cdcai. Medium severity appropriate.

#### `migration-6` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Inventory of what does NOT migrate automatically (and confirmed non-issues: no custom secrets, no branch protection to replicate)

- **Location:** cdcai/TowerScout repo settings + .github/workflows/*.yml
- **Evidence:** Does not move with git push: (1) Actions run history/logs (CI-green evidence for PR #46 stays on the fork); (2) PR history #1-#46 — commit messages containing '(#NN)' will hyperlink to cdcai's nonexistent/wrong PR numbers when browsed in cdcai (cosmetic, unavoidable); (3) Issues — moreover cdcai has has_issues:false (issues DISABLED) per repo API, so users cannot file bugs there until the owner enables them; (4) wiki content (cdcai has_wiki:true but wikis are separate git repos); (5) branch protection — none exists on either side (cdcai branches API: main "protected": false), so nothing to replicate but owner may want to add it; (6) collaborators; (7) Actions secrets/variables — confirmed NON-issue: `grep -o 'secrets\.[A-Za-z_]*' .github/workflows/*.yml` yields only secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN (3 hits: container-publish.yml:39, plus ci.yml and task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml) — no custom secrets to recreate; (8) GHCR packages (covered in migration-3); (9) cdcai currently has no .github/workflows at all (contents API 404, actions/workflows total_count 0) and whether org-level Actions policy permits workflow_dispatch + GITHUB_TOKEN packages:write must be confirmed by the owner.

Keep the fork alive (not deleted) at least until cdcai CI + release are verified, preserving PR/Actions history as an auditable archive.
- **Migration relevance:** Sets correct expectations for the handoff conversation with the cdcai owner; two items need owner action (enable Issues, confirm Actions/GHCR org policy), the rest are accept-and-document.
- **Verifier's note:** Substantively reproduced: cdcai repo API has_issues:false, has_wiki:true; cdcai branches API main protected:false and fork main protected:false (nothing to replicate); cdcai .github/workflows contents API → 404 and actions/workflows total_count=0; secrets grep across .github/workflows/*.yml yields only secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN, 3 hits total — no custom secrets; commits on main contain '(#3)'/'(#4)' refs that would mislink in cdcai. One trivial evidence error: the 3 hits are container-publish.yml:39 plus task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:107 and :195 — ci.yml has ZERO secrets references, contrary to the evidence text's attribution. This mis-attribution does not change the claim or severity (medium stands).

#### `migration-8` · INFO · CONFIRMED — Step-ordered migration runbook (Jonathan vs cdcai owner) with verification checklist

- **Location:** runbook draft (this finding); commands reference local repo c:/Users/Jonat/Documents/TowerScout/TowerScout
- **Evidence:** PHASE 0 — Jonathan, on the fork (by 2026-07-09): 0.1 `gh auth login` (gh 2.92.0 present, currently logged out). 0.2 Merge or close draft PR #46; finalize main. 0.3 Resolve stale v0.1.0 tag (migration-5) and choose the stable version. 0.4 Commit the ghcr.io/j-schulein -> ghcr.io/cdcai default changes (compose.yaml:3, scripts/lib/TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117, scripts/package-release.ps1:37). 0.5 `git remote add cdcai https://github.com/cdcai/TowerScout.git; git fetch cdcai; git checkout main; git merge cdcai/main` (resolve README.md keeping the Enterprise links), `git push origin main`. 0.6 Tag stable: `git tag -a <stable> -m ... <sha>; git push origin <stable>`. PHASE 1 — cdcai owner (Chris Edens): grant Jonathan collaborator write on cdcai/TowerScout; confirm Actions enabled and org allows GITHUB_TOKEN packages:write; agree merge-commit-only policy if PR fallback is used. PHASE 2 — Jonathan: `git push cdcai main` (now fast-forward); `git push cdcai <stable> v0.1.0-rc7.1` (selective; never --tags). PHASE 3 — Jonathan in cdcai: dispatch container-publish (inputs tag=<stable>, pytorch_flavor=cpu, then cuda121) OR `crane copy` the validated digests; owner sets ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout package visibility to Public; fresh clone `git clone https://github.com/cdcai/TowerScout ts-cdcai`; rebuild package: `pwsh scripts/package-release.ps1 -Version <stable> -Image ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout@<digest> ...`; publish: `gh release create <stable> --repo cdcai/TowerScout ... dist/*.zip dist/*.sha256 <assets>` (~800.9 MB, 6 assets — under per-asset limits). PHASE 4 — verification checklist: (a) in the fresh cdcai clone `git describe --tags` == <stable> and `git cat-file -t <source_ref SHA from any shipped SOURCE.txt>` succeeds (proves AGPL corresponding-source intact); (b) unzip cdcai-built ZIP and the J-Schulein rc-stable ZIP side by side and hash-compare file-by-file — byte-identical ZIPs are impossible (package-release.ps1:421 embeds Generated UTC), so expect diffs confined to release-manifest.v1.json/IMAGE.txt/SOURCE.txt/SHA256SUMS.txt lines carrying image ref, digest, and timestamps; every other file hash must match; (c) download cdcai release assets and verify against .sha256 sidecars; (d) anonymous `docker pull ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout@<digest>` from a logged-out session; (e) end-to-end `setup-towerscout.cmd` smoke on a clean space-free path per the rc7.1 harness (Docker CPU minimum). PHASE 5 — both: fork README banner pointing to cdcai; owner enables Issues and optional branch protection; retain (do not delete) the fork until Phase 4 passes.
- **Recommendation:** Convert this skeleton into docs/handoff-runbook.md (or the handoff email) with named owners and dates; schedule Phase 0 completion by 2026-07-09 to leave buffer for the 2026-07-10 target.
- **Migration relevance:** Single execution artifact the team committed to; sequences the divergence fix, image republish, package rebuild-in-cdcai, and release recreation so nothing is discovered at the deadline.
- **Verifier's note:** Runbook finding — every independently checkable fact embedded in it reproduces: gh 2.92.0 installed but logged out; the three ghcr.io/j-schulein default locations (compose.yaml:3, TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117, package-release.ps1:37); stale v0.1.0 tag; merge base and divergence making Phase-2 push a fast-forward after the Phase-0 merge; package-release.ps1:421 embeds Generated UTC (so byte-identical ZIP comparison is correctly ruled out and the diff-confined-to-manifest expectation is sound); PR #46 confirmed open draft, base main, 30 commits; cdcai has zero workflows/releases/tags so Phases 1/3 owner actions are necessary; rc7.1 release = 6 assets / 800.9 MB, all under per-asset limits. Sequencing is consistent with findings 1-6. Info severity appropriate.

### 6.3 Tags & releases

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited all 15 local tags, 14 origin tags, and all 13 GitHub releases on J-Schulein/TowerScout (via git tag/cat-file/merge-base, git ls-remote, and the GitHub releases API), plus upstream cdcai/TowerScout (0 tags, 0 releases — clean slate, so nothing transfers automatically and nothing collides upstream). Note the release inventory is larger than the briefing stated: rc1–rc4 and rc5-candidate.3 also have GitHub prereleases, for 13 total; all are prerelease=true/draft=false, so no 'Latest' release exists anywhere yet. Tag/release cross-check: every GitHub release has a matching tag; the only tags without releases are v0.1.0 (on origin, no release — the stale 2025-12-04 'Security Foundation, 15% complete' milestone tag, the top finding) and uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 (local-only, its commit unreachable from any remote branch). The 800MB assets ZIP was proven byte-identical across all 13 releases by downloading every .sha256 sidecar (all 00599cc4fe9f...), so exactly one copy needs to migrate. Things that checked out fine: rc7 is properly labeled '(superseded)'; the rc-tag lineage rc1→rc7.1 is chronologically sane and all rc tags except rc5-candidate.3 point to commits on main; internal release-package hashes in the rc7.1 body match the published sidecars; and the rc7.1 release notes accurately describe the manual TLS repair requirement (repair-provider-tls.cmd flow fully documented in the tagged docs/quick-start.md). Main handoff risks: the v0.1.0 tag name collision with the planned stable release, ~10.4 GB of duplicated release assets that must NOT be bulk-migrated, ad-hoc validation tags (three of which pin commits that exist only on feature/task-086-provider-tls-repair) that should stay behind or be archive/-namespaced, and release notes pinning images to the personal ghcr.io/j-schulein namespace.

#### `tags-releases-1` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Stale annotated tag v0.1.0 (2025-12-04) collides with the planned stable v0.1.0 release name

- **Location:** tag v0.1.0 (tag object f00e870, target commit e78974d) — exists locally AND on origin
- **Evidence:** git cat-file -p v0.1.0 shows an annotated tag, tagger 'Jonathan Schulein <Schulein_Jonathan@bah.com>', dated 1764862147 (2025-12-04), message 'TowerScout v0.1.0: Security Foundation Complete ... Project Status: 15% complete with solid security foundation established / Next Phase: Error Handling Infrastructure (TASK-003)'. Target commit e78974d 'chore: cleanup test files and improve gitignore' (2025-12-04) is on origin/main. git ls-remote --tags origin confirms refs/tags/v0.1.0 exists on the GitHub fork. GitHub releases API shows NO release for tag v0.1.0. Meanwhile v0.1.0-rc1..rc7.1 (2026-05-29 through 2026-06-29) all postdate it — so the repo currently claims 'v0.1.0' shipped six months before its own release candidates.
- **Recommendation:** Before cutting the stable release: delete the tag from origin (git push origin :refs/tags/v0.1.0) and locally (git tag -d v0.1.0); if the milestone is worth keeping, retag the same commit as e.g. archive/milestone-security-foundation-2025-12-04 first. Then either reuse v0.1.0 for the stable release or (safer, avoids any cached-tag confusion for people who already fetched the fork) name the stable release v0.1.1 or v1.0.0. Do NOT push this tag to cdcai/TowerScout.
- **Migration relevance:** The team must publish a stable release by 2026-07-10/13. If that release is named v0.1.0, `git tag v0.1.0` will fail locally and `git push` will be rejected on any repo that already has this tag; worse, a bulk `git push --tags` to cdcai/TowerScout during migration would plant this misleading 15%-complete milestone tag upstream as the apparent stable v0.1.0. Anyone checking out v0.1.0 would get a December 2025 pre-alpha tree.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced fully. `git cat-file -p v0.1.0` shows annotated tag object f00e870ca525, tagger Jonathan Schulein <Schulein_Jonathan@bah.com>, timestamp 1764862147 (Dec 4 2025), message 'TowerScout v0.1.0: Security Foundation Complete ... Project Status: 15% complete ... Next Phase: Error Handling Infrastructure (TASK-003)'. Target e78974d ('chore: cleanup test files and improve gitignore', Thu Dec 4 10:28:24 2025) is an ancestor of origin/main (merge-base --is-ancestor passes). `git ls-remote --tags origin` lists refs/tags/v0.1.0 -> f00e870 (peeled e78974d), so the stale tag IS on the GitHub fork. Releases API returns 13 releases, none with tag_name v0.1.0. All RC tags/releases (rc1 2026-05-29 through rc7.1 2026-06-29) postdate it. Since the entire RC line is v0.1.0-rc*, v0.1.0 is the natural stable-release name and the collision is real: local `git tag v0.1.0` would fail and pushing a re-pointed v0.1.0 would be rejected; a bulk --tags push during migration would plant the misleading Dec-2025 '15% complete' tag upstream. High severity is justified for the handoff.

#### `tags-releases-2` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Identical 800 MB assets ZIP duplicated on all 13 GitHub releases (~10.4 GB); only one copy needs to migrate

- **Location:** https://api.github.com/repos/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases — asset *-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip on every release
- **Evidence:** Releases API: every one of the 13 releases (v0.1.0-rc1..rc7.1, rc5-candidate.3, gpu-validation-2026-06-16, 3x tls-validation-2026-06-26*) carries an assets ZIP of exactly 800,655,295 bytes. Downloading each release's .sha256 sidecar shows the identical hash on all 13: 00599cc4fe9f2bdb4708c669d7c3d9a8a570a0c3b547bc5c317026196c7bacbb — byte-identical file, ~10.4 GB total duplicated storage. The file is the model/asset bundle dated 2026-05-05 and has not changed since rc1.
- **Recommendation:** Migrate exactly ONE copy of the assets ZIP: attach it (plus its .sha256) to the stable release created in cdcai/TowerScout, and to v0.1.0-rc7.1 if that release is also recreated upstream. Verify the upload against sha256 00599cc4fe9f2bdb4708c669d7c3d9a8a570a0c3b547bc5c317026196c7bacbb. Leave the other 12 copies behind in the fork; optionally strip the assets ZIP from superseded fork prereleases to cut ~9.6 GB, but that is cosmetic.
- **Migration relevance:** GitHub releases do not transfer to the upstream repo when handing back a fork; every asset must be re-uploaded. Re-uploading 13 copies of the same 800 MB file to cdcai/TowerScout would be wasteful and slow; conversely, forgetting the assets ZIP entirely would make the migrated release non-functional (packages require the shared asset bundle).
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. Releases API shows all 13 releases carry an *-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip of exactly 800,655,295 bytes. I downloaded the .sha256 sidecar from every one of the 13 releases: all report the identical hash 00599cc4fe9f2bdb4708c669d7c3d9a8a570a0c3b547bc5c317026196c7bacbb. 13 x 800,655,295 = 10,408,518,835 bytes (~10.4 GB) of duplicated storage. Migration relevance is sound: fork releases do not transfer upstream, packages require the shared asset ZIP (rc7.1 release lists it as a required asset with sidecar validation), and only one copy needs re-upload. Medium severity appropriate.

#### `tags-releases-3` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Ad-hoc validation tags/releases (tls-validation x3, gpu-validation, rc5-candidate.3) should not migrate; tls-validation commits are not on main

- **Location:** tags tls-validation-2026-06-26{,-V2,-V3}, gpu-validation-2026-06-16, v0.1.0-rc5-candidate.3 + their GitHub releases
- **Evidence:** git branch -r --contains shows the three tls-validation target commits (1566163, 280b073, cd362ec — all 'task-086' commits from 2026-06-26) are reachable ONLY from origin/feature/task-086-provider-tls-repair, NOT origin/main (git merge-base --is-ancestor returns NO for all three). v0.1.0-rc5-candidate.3 (ef6904f) is likewise NOT on main. All five have corresponding PRERELEASE GitHub releases, each dragging the 800 MB assets ZIP (see tags-releases-2). These were one-off tester/validation builds: rc5-candidate.3 was superseded by rc5 the next day; the tls-validation trio was superseded by rc7/rc7.1 which merged the task-086 work to main.
- **Recommendation:** Do NOT recreate any of these in cdcai/TowerScout. In the fork, either leave them untouched (they document the validation trail referenced in UAT records) or, if the fork itself is being tidied for reference, rename to an archive/ namespace (git tag archive/tls-validation-2026-06-26 <sha> etc., then delete originals). If origin/feature/task-086-provider-tls-repair is pruned during branch cleanup, keep the tls-validation tags OR the branch — not neither — so the validated SHAs in the UAT evidence remain resolvable.
- **Migration relevance:** Migrating these to cdcai/TowerScout would clutter the new owner's tag namespace with internal debugging artifacts and, for the tls-validation tags, would carry commits from an unmerged feature branch into upstream. Left as-is in the fork they are harmless (tags keep their commits alive even if the feature branch is later deleted).
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. `git branch -r --contains` for 1566163a, 280b073a, cd362ec9 each returns ONLY origin/feature/task-086-provider-tls-repair; merge-base --is-ancestor vs origin/main fails for all three, plus for rc5-candidate.3 (ef6904fc, contained only in origin/feature/task-083-rc5-podman-independence-gpu-release). All five tags have prerelease GitHub releases each carrying the 800,655,295-byte assets ZIP. Supersession timeline verified: rc5-candidate.3 published 2026-06-15T23:12:06Z, rc5 published 2026-06-16T15:00:54Z (next day); tls-validation trio published 2026-06-26, rc7/rc7.1 published 2026-06-29 from main. Recommendation (don't migrate; keep tags or branch so validated SHAs stay resolvable) is correct git semantics. Medium appropriate as migration guidance.

#### `tags-releases-4` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — rc7.1 release notes pin container images under the personal ghcr.io/j-schulein namespace

- **Location:** https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases/tag/v0.1.0-rc7.1 — Artifact Identity table in release body
- **Evidence:** rc7.1 release body pins: 'ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:v0.1.0-rc7.1-cpu@sha256:14b6ef52...' and 'ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:v0.1.0-rc7.1-cuda121@sha256:95f1f396...'. These GHCR packages live under the departing contributor's personal GitHub namespace, not under cdcai.
- **Recommendation:** For the stable release, publish the CPU/CUDA images to a namespace the new owner controls (ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout) and write the stable release notes against those digests; alternatively obtain an explicit commitment that ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout stays public, and record that in the handoff notes. Flag this to whichever dimension owns container-registry migration.
- **Migration relevance:** If the stable release (and any recreated rc7.1) in cdcai/TowerScout copies this release-notes format, its image references will point at a personal registry namespace that the new owner does not control and that may be deleted or made private after the project ends 2026-07-15, silently breaking the documented install path.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. rc7.1 release body Artifact Identity table pins exactly `ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:v0.1.0-rc7.1-cpu@sha256:14b6ef523f93a91bbcceef4163b2d100a3b8c3f0b32bfdc6b91c362694ae3d09` and `ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:v0.1.0-rc7.1-cuda121@sha256:95f1f3967294957543ed0c40e11531a5af2d56f2beb7723973596b952fc39ffd` — personal namespace, not cdcai. If anything the finding UNDERSTATES scope: at HEAD 533e18b the personal namespace is also the default image in compose.yaml line 3 (`image: ${TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE:-ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:latest-cpu}`), .env.example line 5, and scripts/package-release.ps1 line 37 — so the registry-migration dependency is repo-wide, not just release-notes cosmetics. Medium stands (the finding correctly defers the broader registry migration to another dimension).

#### `tags-releases-5` · LOW · CONFIRMED — Local-only tag uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 points at a commit unreachable from any remote branch — bulk-push hazard

- **Location:** local tag uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 (commit 64c2b5d) in c:/Users/Jonat/Documents/TowerScout/TowerScout
- **Evidence:** git tag lists uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 locally, but git ls-remote --tags origin does not include it — it is the only local tag absent from origin. Its commit 64c2b5d 'fix(task-083): Podman 5.8.2 provider guardrail + GPU VMType gate (UAT PR #33)' (2026-06-15) is NOT an ancestor of origin/main and `git branch -r --contains 64c2b5d` returns nothing — the commit exists only in this clone, kept alive solely by the tag (the fix itself landed via the merged PR #33 line, per rc5 history).
- **Recommendation:** Delete it locally before any tag-push migration step (git tag -d uat-local-fix-2026-06-15), or explicitly push tags one-by-one by name during migration instead of using --tags/--mirror.
- **Migration relevance:** Harmless while local, but a naive migration step like `git push cdcai --tags` or `git push --mirror` would upload this throwaway UAT tag and its orphan commit into the upstream repo.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. `git tag` lists uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 locally; `git ls-remote --tags origin` does NOT include it (only 15 local tags exist and it is the sole one absent from origin). Commit 64c2b5dd3b6a ('fix(task-083): Podman 5.8.2 provider guardrail + GPU VMType gate (UAT PR #33)', 2026-06-15) is not an ancestor of origin/main and both `git branch --contains` and `git branch -r --contains` return empty — the commit is kept alive solely by this local tag. Bulk `git push --tags`/`--mirror` to cdcai would indeed upload it plus its orphan commit. Low severity correct (local-only hazard, easy to avoid).

### 6.4 Branches

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited all 19 remote branches on origin, 4 local branches, and tag/stash/tracking-ref state of C:\Users\Jonat\Documents\TowerScout\TowerScout ahead of the cdcai/TowerScout handoff. Method: because the repo uses squash merges, 'git branch --merged' was ignored; each branch was tested with (a) git merge-tree --write-tree vs origin/main tree 88c3871a, (b) git cherry / rev-list ahead-behind counts, (c) tip-tree equality against the corresponding squash commit in main, and (d) the authoritative GitHub PR list (46 PRs, states fetched via API). Overall health is good: every remote branch except the live PR #46 head is fully merged or superseded — exact tip-tree matches were proven for feature-sprint-05 (== squash 3417c64), task-083 (== squash daf7f62), task-086 (== squash 96a5faf), task-087-helper-transport (== origin/main tip 229f59f), and docs/towerscout-codex-skills (tip tree identical to the merged -clean variant); checkpoint/pre-task-083-rc4 is a plain ancestor of main and exactly equals tag v0.1.0-rc4. No branch requires TAG-THEN-DELETE: the only genuinely unique unmerged content anywhere is a 41-line WIP handoff note on feature/task-087-wip whose action items were since completed in PR #46, plus 3 stale Dec-2025/Jan-2026 docs-maintenance commits shared by 'improvements'/'geocoding-system-integration'. Things checked that are fine: remote-tracking refs are current (git ls-remote heads match refs/remotes/origin exactly), git stash is empty, all local tags except uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 exist on origin and vice versa, local pr-46 is byte-identical to the PR #46 head 533e18b, and local feature/task-083-rc5-... is in sync with its (merged) remote. Net action: after PR #46 merges, 18 of 19 remote branches can be deleted with zero content loss, leaving only main — a clean single-branch handoff. (Out of scope for this dimension but observed: worktree has untracked .env.uat-backup-20260615 and .agent_work/evidence/ — flag to the working-tree/secrets dimension.)

### 6.5 Packaging & release build

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited the release-packaging path at pr-46 HEAD 533e18b. Overall the code-ZIP path is in good shape: scripts/package-release.ps1 is a strict 64-file allowlist (Copy-ReleaseItem throws on any missing file), so .env, .env.backup.*, .towerscout-runtime/, helper tokens/logs (written at runtime under <package>/.towerscout-runtime/host-helper per TowerScoutHostHelper.ps1:142), .agent_work/, .agents/, .github/, and tests/ are excluded by construction — verified empirically by inspecting the published rc7.1 CPU control ZIP (65 entries, no leakage, dotfile .env.example correctly included despite the Compress-Archive wildcard). The new PR-46 files ARE in the allowlist: scripts/lib/TowerScoutCertificateStore.ps1 (line 266, the only allowlist addition vs main) and host-helper.cmd/.ps1/host-helper-visible.cmd (lines 276-278, already on main via commit 229f59f which is one of the 2 commits past the rc7.1 tag). All 64 allowlist entries are git-tracked on both main and HEAD. Guardrails are solid: requires sha256 image digest, git source ref, and clean working tree by default, with explicit -Allow* overrides that print 'local validation only' warnings; generates IMAGE.txt, SOURCE.txt, SBOM.txt, release-manifest.v1.json, SHA256SUMS.txt, and a ZIP .sha256 sidecar, and rewrites the staged .env.example with the pinned digest. release-manifest.v1.json exists in two forms: the repo-root file is a checked-in TEMPLATE (release_version: 'template') consumed by Dockerfile:59 (copied into the image), webapp/towerscout.py:94 (/license payload), tests/unit/test_release_manifest_schema.py, and the .agents release-gate checker; the generated per-package manifest is consumed at setup time by scripts/lib/TowerScoutBootstrap.ps1 (asset-ZIP filename enforcement at ~line 883, IMAGE.txt/version and .env digest cross-checks at lines 1039/1058) and TowerScoutCompose.ps1:179. rc7.1 reality matches the script and the asset-bundle contract exactly: GitHub release v0.1.0-rc7.1 carries towerscout-v0.1.0-rc7.1-{cpu,cuda121}.zip(+.sha256) and towerscout-v0.1.0-rc7.1-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip (800.7MB)(+.sha256); the ZIP's manifest has populated digest sha256:14b6ef52..., asset_bundle_sha256 00599cc4..., and source_ref 1152c16 == the v0.1.0-rc7.1 tag commit. dist/ is gitignored (.gitignore:7) and untracked; only stale local rc5 outputs sit there (cosmetic). Tests (tests/unit/test_release_package_script.py, 292 lines, 4 tests) genuinely execute the script and assert digest enforcement, dirty-tree rejection, full staged content including CertificateStore/repair-tls/docs, manifest schema via release_manifest_contract.assert_manifest_schema, CPU/CUDA shared asset-bundle identity, and flavor validation — but they are Windows-only and CI is ubuntu-latest, so they never run in CI. Versioning is clean: NO version bump is needed anywhere in-repo to cut stable v0.1.0 — the version flows entirely from the container-publish workflow tag input and package-release -Version/-AssetBundleVersion parameters (package.json '2.6.1' is upstream legacy, unused; the UI/webapp embeds no version string). THE EXPLICIT REBUILD ANSWER: cdcai CAN rebuild the code ZIPs and container images from the repo alone (documented end-to-end in docs/support/oci-quick-start.md 'Creating A Release Package' + 'Publishing The GHCR Image'; container-publish.yml portably uses ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}), but CANNOT rebuild the ~800MB assets ZIP from the repo alone: the model weights (model_params/ is gitignored) exist only as GitHub Release assets on this fork, no script assembles the asset ZIP, and no doc states where the weights or the Census shapefile originals live. SBOM.txt is hand-maintained placeholder text in both the repo root and the generated package; no SBOM generation tooling exists anywhere, despite the generated manifest declaring one 'must be generated or attached for each release candidate'.

#### `packaging-1` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Asset bundle (~800MB, model weights + Census data) is not rebuildable from the repo; sole source is the fork's own GitHub Release assets

- **Location:** docs/release/release-asset-bundle-contract.md; webapp/asset_manifest.v1.json; .gitignore (model_params/ ignored)
- **Evidence:** No script in the repo assembles the assets ZIP (grep for assembly/Compress-Archive across docs+scripts only finds the CONTROL-package assembly in docs/support/oci-quick-start.md:114). .gitignore excludes 'model_params/' and 'webapp/model_params/*', so model_params/yolov5/newest.pt (175,084,429 bytes, sha256 27315E15...) and model_params/EN/b5_unweighted_best.pt (118,567,303 bytes, sha256 645113BA...) are pinned by hash in webapp/asset_manifest.v1.json but exist nowhere in git. MODEL_LICENSES.md documents license posture but no acquisition source; DATA_LICENSES.md names 'US Census TIGER/Line ZCTA' but gives no download URL (grep -i 'download|obtain|census.gov' DATA_LICENSES.md returns nothing). The manifest's own 'recovery' field is circular: 'Import the release asset bundle'. The only place the bundle exists is the fork's releases: GitHub API shows towerscout-v0.1.0-rc7.1-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip (800.7MB) attached to https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases (same bundle content on rc6/rc7 releases). Model/ contains training notebooks (EN_classifier_train.ipynb, w210_model_NB*.ipynb) but retraining cannot reproduce the exact pinned hashes.
- **Recommendation:** Before handoff: (a) attach the asset ZIP (renamed towerscout-v0.1.0-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip with regenerated sidecar) to the stable release so it transfers with the repo/release history; (b) add a short 'Asset bundle assembly' section (or a small script) documenting how to stage assets/ and produce the ZIP + sidecar; (c) record in MODEL_LICENSES.md or a handoff doc the authoritative custody location of the weight files and the Census TIGER/Line download URL (www2.census.gov TIGER2025 ZCTA520), and explicitly hand the bundle (or a copy) to cdcai.
- **Migration relevance:** This is the core 'can cdcai republish' gap. If the fork's Releases are deleted or not transferred at handoff, the exact-hash asset bundle is unrecoverable and every future release (whose setup flow enforces manifest hashes via TowerScoutBootstrap.ps1) is blocked. Renaming for a new version also has no tooling: the asset ZIP name embeds the release version (towerscout-<release-version>-assets-<manifest-version>.zip) so cutting stable v0.1.0 requires renaming the rc7.1 bundle and regenerating its .sha256 sidecar by hand per the contract's line format.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced all core claims: only Compress-Archive in the repo is the control package (scripts/package-release.ps1:540) — no asset-bundle assembly script exists; .gitignore:59-60 ignore model_params/ and webapp/model_params/*; 'git ls-files' shows no .pt files tracked, and model_params/ does not even exist in this working tree; webapp/asset_manifest.v1.json pins newest.pt (175,084,429 bytes, sha256 27315E15...) and b5_unweighted_best.pt (118,567,303 bytes, sha256 645113BA...) with the circular recovery text 'Import the release asset bundle...'; MODEL_LICENSES.md documents license posture only (no weight-file acquisition/custody source) and DATA_LICENSES.md names Census TIGER/Line ZCTA with no download URL; GitHub API confirms the 800,655,295-byte bundle towerscout-v0.1.0-rc7.1-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip on the fork's releases (identical size back to rc1); setup enforces the exact release-version-embedding bundle filename (TowerScoutBootstrap.ps1 ~1097-1100 throws "Asset ZIP filename '<actual>' does not match expected release artifact '<expected>'"), so cutting v0.1.0 requires a manual rename + regenerated sidecar per the contract's line format (docs/release/release-asset-bundle-contract.md:142-151). One caveat that does not change the verdict: upstream cdcai/TowerScout's README lists Google Drive links for YOLOv5/EfficientNet weights and a census.gov ZCTA link (TIGER2019), so 'sole source' is marginally overstated — but those Drive files are unverified against the pinned sha256s, the Census vintage differs (fork uses tl_2025_us_zcta520), and nothing in this repo documents them, so the exact-hash bundle is in practice recoverable only from the fork's releases. High severity stands for the handoff.

### 6.6 CI workflows

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited all 3 workflow files in .github/workflows/ at HEAD 533e18b (branch pr-46), plus their supporting scripts (scripts/ci_publish_issue.py, scripts/ci_start_services.sh), git history, and live GitHub API state.

WORKFLOW INVENTORY: (1) ci.yml "TowerScout CI/CD Testing Pipeline" — push/PR to main; jobs: frontend-test (Node 18, bundle rebuild, ProviderStateManager regression), test (Python 3.11/3.12 matrix: flake8, black, mypy, bandit, pytest unit+integration, codecov, artifacts), build (docker build, main-only, advisory), security (Trivy fs scan + SARIF upload). Identical to origin/main. (2) container-publish.yml — workflow_dispatch-only GHCR publish, cpu/cuda121 flavors; clean and fully portable (image name derived from ${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}, only secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN, SHA-pinned actions, set -euo pipefail). (3) task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml — net-new in PR #46 (upstream cdcai/TowerScout has NO .github/workflows at all, verified via API 'Not Found'); 2 jobs (puppeteer-templates, puppeteer-e2e with simulated helper); triggers pull_request:['**'] + push to feature branch and main + workflow_dispatch.

THINGS THAT ARE FINE (checked, no finding): the old 'tee masks e2e' hole IS fixed at HEAD — commit 2b67123 added defaults.run.shell: bash, which makes GitHub Actions invoke bash with -eo pipefail, so the three remaining `node ... | tee` steps now propagate failures; the main ci.yml contains no `| tee` patterns at all; no hardcoded repo slugs, user handles, gist IDs, or issue numbers exist in any workflow or CI script (ci_publish_issue.py resolves repo/PR from GITHUB_REPOSITORY/GITHUB_REF/event payload); the only secret referenced anywhere is secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN (container-publish.yml:39, task-087:107,195) — no custom secrets to recreate in cdcai; all jobs run on ubuntu-latest, no self-hosted runner assumptions; ci.yml and container-publish.yml permissions blocks are least-privilege (contents:read; +security-events:write for Trivy; +packages:write for publish); Trivy action is SHA-pinned and needs no external config on a public repo. CI at HEAD is genuinely green (runs at 2026-07-07T22:02 success for both workflows).

MAIN PROBLEM: the task-087 workflow still carries heavy PR-46 debugging residue — most seriously the 'Publish test logs as repo issue' steps in BOTH jobs, whose fallback chain is actively spamming PR #46 with 11 github-actions[bot] full-log-dump comments and would start creating 2 repo issues per run on any future repo where Issues are enabled (issue creation currently fails only because the fork — and today upstream — has has_issues=False). Plus: no permissions block, duplicate push+PR runs, tag-pinned actions and npx playwright@latest, diagnostics/apt-workaround residue.

RECOMMENDED PRE-HANDOFF CI CLEANUP LIST (in order): 1) delete both 'Publish ... logs as repo issue' steps and scripts/ci_publish_issue.py (artifact upload already preserves logs); 2) add `permissions: contents: read` to task-087 workflow; 3) narrow triggers to pull_request→main + push→main (drop feature-branch push and the '**' glob), or fold both jobs into ci.yml and delete the task-specific file; 4) SHA-pin checkout/setup-node/upload-artifact and pin playwright to an exact version; 5) remove the two 'Diagnostics' steps and dedupe/drop the apt-cache-policy loops; 6) document in the handoff notes which ci.yml gates are advisory (continue-on-error) so cdcai knows what green means; 7) (out of CI scope but observed) README.md:22 hardcodes https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases — fix during migration.

#### `ci-1` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Task-087 workflow publishes CI test logs as repo issues / PR comments / gists on every run — active PR-46 debugging residue that must be stripped before handoff

- **Location:** .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:105-110 and 193-198; scripts/ci_publish_issue.py
- **Evidence:** Both jobs end with a step 'Publish test logs as repo issue' / 'Publish e2e logs as repo issue' running `python3 scripts/ci_publish_issue.py test-artifacts` with GITHUB_TOKEN. The script (scripts/ci_publish_issue.py:167) POSTs a new issue to /repos/{repo}/issues with the full contents of every file in test-artifacts (20KB per file); on 'Issues has been disabled' it falls back to posting a PR comment (line 189), then GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY, then a private gist (line 217). Live proof the fallback fires: GET /repos/J-Schulein/TowerScout/issues/46/comments returns 11 comments, ALL from github-actions[bot], each beginning 'Repository: J-Schulein/TowerScout Workflow: Task-087 Frontend Puppetee...' (timestamps 2026-07-06T22:18Z through 2026-07-07T22:03Z, up to 2 per push — one per job). Issue creation itself currently 410s only because the fork has has_issues=False (verified: GET /repos/J-Schulein/TowerScout → has_issues: False). Added by commits 13752ba 'publish Puppeteer test logs as repo issue for easier triage' and 55095fb 'add publish fallbacks (PR comment, gist, actions summary)' — explicitly triage tooling. Also note the gist fallback is dead code: the built-in GITHUB_TOKEN has no gist scope, so create_gist always 403s.
- **Recommendation:** Before merging PR #46: delete the two 'Publish ... logs as repo issue' steps and scripts/ci_publish_issue.py entirely. The 'Upload test artifacts' steps (actions/upload-artifact) already preserve the same logs for triage. If any publishing is truly wanted, keep only the GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY write.
- **Migration relevance:** After transfer to cdcai/TowerScout, every PR (trigger is pull_request: branches ['**']) and every push to main runs both jobs and attempts to publish logs: 2 new repo issues per run if cdcai ever enables Issues (upstream has_issues is currently False, but the CDC org may enable it), otherwise log-dump PR comments on every contributor PR. That is unprofessional noise handed to the new owner and leaks full CI logs into issue/comment history.
- **Verifier's note:** Fully reproduced. Publish steps exist at task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:105-110 and 193-198, both running scripts/ci_publish_issue.py with secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN. Script verified at HEAD 533e18b: POSTs a new repo issue with full artifact contents 20KB/file (create_issue call at line 167), falls back to a PR comment on 'Issues has been disabled' (create_pr_comment at line 189), then GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY, then a gist (create_gist at line 217). Live evidence reproduced: GET /repos/J-Schulein/TowerScout returns has_issues: False, and GET issues/46/comments returns exactly 11 comments, all github-actions[bot], all starting 'Repository: J-Schulein/TowerScout\nWorkflow: Task-087 Puppetee...', timestamps 2026-07-06T22:18:15Z through 2026-07-07T22:03:25Z (two per recent push, one per job). Commits 13752ba ('publish Puppeteer test logs as repo issue for easier triage') and 55095fb ('add publish fallbacks (PR comment, gist, actions summary)') verified — explicit triage/debugging tooling. Gist-dead-code claim correct (Actions installation token has no gist scope; additionally the gist path is unreachable in Actions because the GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY write succeeds first, setting fallback_handled). High severity justified: triggered on pull_request branches ['**'] and push to main, so post-transfer it would spam log-dump PR comments or repo issues in cdcai/TowerScout — active debugging residue that must be stripped before handoff; upload-artifact steps already preserve the same logs.

#### `ci-2` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml has no permissions block — runs with repo-default token permissions while intentionally performing write operations

- **Location:** .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml (whole file; compare ci.yml:9-10)
- **Evidence:** grep for 'permissions' across .github/workflows: ci.yml:9 (contents: read), ci.yml:151 (contents: read, security-events: write), container-publish.yml:24 (contents: read, packages: write) — task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml has zero permissions blocks. Its issue/PR-comment publishing steps (lines 105-110, 193-198) require issues:write / pull-requests:write, so today it depends on the repository's default GITHUB_TOKEN setting being write-permissive, and the successful bot PR comments on PR #46 prove the default token IS write-capable in this fork.

Either way it is the one workflow in the repo violating the otherwise consistent least-privilege convention.

Its publish steps (lines 105-110, 193-198) intentionally perform issues:write / pull-requests:write API calls, and the 11 successful bot comments on PR #46 prove the repo's default GITHUB_TOKEN is currently write-capable. The script always returns 0, so under a read-only org default it would break silently. Medium is right: it is the one workflow violating the repo's otherwise consistent least-privilege convention, largely mooted if the ci-1 publish steps are deleted (then only 'permissions: contents: read' needs adding).

#### `ci-3` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Task-087 workflow triggers cause duplicate runs per commit and contain a dead feature-branch trigger post-merge

- **Migration relevance:** Doubles CI minutes on every push to an open PR; after PR #46 merges, the feature-branch push trigger is permanently dead residue; ['**'] means every PR against any base branch runs a task-named workflow the new owner has no context for.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. Triggers verified at task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:3-10: pull_request branches ['**'], push to [feature/task-087-gate3-product-integration, main], plus workflow_dispatch. GET /actions/runs shows the same commit running twice: SHA 533e18b3 as push at 2026-07-07T22:02:11Z and pull_request at 22:02:14Z; SHA 2b67123c at 21:54:35Z/21:54:36Z; same for bca6341d and 6c05b09e. Commit 5b5a1ed 'ci(task-087): add workflow_dispatch to allow manual runs' verified as debugging addition. Workflow/job names are task-numbered ('Task-087 Frontend Puppeteer Templates', 'Run Puppeteer templates (Task-087)'). Post-merge the feature-branch push trigger is dead, and ['**'] runs the workflow on every PR against any base. Medium severity appropriate for a clean handoff.

#### `ci-4` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Task-087 workflow uses tag-pinned actions and unpinned `npx playwright@latest`, inconsistent with the repo's SHA-pinning convention; Node 18 is EOL

- **Location:** .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:23,26,59,100,123,154,188; .github/workflows/ci.yml:20-24
- **Evidence:** task-087 uses actions/checkout@v4, actions/setup-node@v4, actions/upload-artifact@v4 (mutable tags), while ci.yml and container-publish.yml SHA-pin every action (e.g. ci.yml:18 'actions/checkout@93cb6efe18208431cddfb8368fd83d5badbf9bfd # v5'). Lines 59 and 154 run `npx -y playwright@latest install --with-deps chromium` — whatever version is latest at run time, on Node 18 (setup-node node-version: '18' at lines 28/124), which has been EOL since 2025-04; ci.yml:20 carries the acknowledging TODO 'Migrate to a supported Node LTS after validating the current build and Puppeteer smoke paths.'
- **Recommendation:** SHA-pin the three actions to match the repo convention and pin playwright to an exact version (e.g. `npx -y playwright@1.x.y install ...`) or add it to devDependencies. Track the Node 18→20/22 migration TODO in the handoff notes if not done before 2026-07-10.
- **Migration relevance:** Playwright regularly drops old-Node support in new releases, so @latest on EOL Node 18 is a time bomb that can turn cdcai's inherited CI red with no code change. Mixed pinning also fails common gov/org supply-chain policies (CDC repos often require pinned actions).
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced with one immaterial line-number nit. task-087 uses mutable tags: checkout@v4 (lines 23, 118), setup-node@v4 (lines 26, 122 — the finding cites 123, actual is 122), upload-artifact@v4 (lines 100, 188), while ci.yml and container-publish.yml SHA-pin every action (e.g. ci.yml:18 checkout@93cb6efe... # v5, ci.yml:113 codecov@ab904c41... # v3). Lines 59 and 154 run `npx -y playwright@latest install --with-deps chromium` — unpinned @latest — under Node 18 (node-version '18' at lines 28 and 124), which reached EOL 2025-04-30; ci.yml:20 carries the acknowledged TODO to migrate off Node 18. Playwright has been deprecating/dropping old-Node support in successive releases, so @latest on EOL Node 18 can turn inherited CI red with no code change. Mixed pinning inconsistent with the repo's own convention and typical gov-org supply-chain policy. Medium confirmed.

#### `ci-5` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Main pipeline 'green' is partly advisory: 7 steps/jobs are continue-on-error, including integration tests and the Docker image build

- **Evidence:** continue-on-error: true on: black format check (line 75), mypy (81), bandit (87), integration tests (110 — 'Don't fail build on integration test failures initially'), codecov upload (118), Docker image build (146 — 'Advisory until Sprint 06 release gates decide whether image build should be merge-blocking'), Trivy scan (164) and SARIF upload (171). That's a deliberate, commented policy — not a bug — but if undocumented, the new owner will over-trust CI status. Also the 'Sprint 06 release gates' comment references internal sprint planning that means nothing upstream.
