Merge-blocking gates are only flake8 E9/F63/F7/F82, unit tests (pytest tests/unit/), and the frontend job (bundle rebuild + one regression test). The tee/pipefail claim verified: `| tee` appears only in the task-087 file (lines 92, 97, 185), and commit 2b67123 added `defaults: run: shell: bash` (GHA bash = -eo pipefail), fixing the prior masking hole at HEAD. Nit: only line 146 references sprint planning; line 75's comment ('Historical formatting drift is advisory...') is already owner-neutral. Documentation-for-handoff recommendation is sound; medium stands.

#### `ci-6` · LOW · CONFIRMED — Debugging residue in task-087 workflow: 'Diagnostics' process-dump steps and a duplicated 20-line apt-cache-policy workaround loop

- **Location:** .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:80-87, 169-178 (diagnostics); 30-49, 126-145 (apt loops)
- **Evidence:** Two `if: always()` 'Diagnostics' steps run `ps aux | egrep 'http-server|node'` / `egrep 'simulated_helper|http-server|node'` and dump log heads — added by commit 61555e5 'add diagnostics steps to capture webserver/helper process and logs for triage' during the CI firefight (see also 213e2ca 'skip virtual-only apt packages using apt-cache policy', 349e473 'make apt installs tolerant on Ubuntu 24'). The identical ~20-line PKGS/apt-cache-policy loop is copy-pasted into both jobs to survive Ubuntu 24.04's t64 package renames (libasound2 → libasound2t64 is skipped as 'not installable'), yet lines 59/154 then run `playwright install --with-deps chromium`, which installs the browser's real dependency set anyway, making most of the manual apt block redundant.
- **Recommendation:** Delete the two Diagnostics steps; try removing the manual apt block entirely and rely on `playwright install --with-deps chromium` (one quick CI run verifies); if some Puppeteer-specific package is still needed, extract the loop into a single reusable script.
- **Migration relevance:** Not a correctness problem (tolerant-by-design), but it is visibly scar tissue from PR-46 debugging; handing cdcai two copies of a workaround loop plus triage dumps makes the workflow harder to trust and maintain.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. Two `if: always()` Diagnostics steps at task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:80-87 (`ps aux | egrep 'http-server|node'` + webserver.log head) and 169-178 (`egrep 'simulated_helper|http-server|node'` + helper/webserver log heads), added by commit 61555e5 'add diagnostics steps to capture webserver/helper process and logs for triage'. The ~20-line PKGS/apt-cache-policy loop is duplicated verbatim at lines 30-49 and 126-145 (commits 349e473, 213e2ca for Ubuntu 24 t64 tolerance). Lines 59/154 then run `playwright install --with-deps chromium`, which installs Chromium's OS dependency set anyway, making most of the manual apt block redundant. Correct characterization as debugging scar tissue, low severity appropriate.

#### `ci-7` · LOW · CONFIRMED — codecov-action v3 is deprecated/sunset — upload silently no-ops or fails, and cdcai would need its own Codecov setup

- **Location:** .github/workflows/ci.yml:112-118
- **Evidence:** Line 113: `uses: codecov/codecov-action@ab904c41d6ece82784817410c45d8b8c02684457 # v3` with `continue-on-error: true` and no CODECOV_TOKEN. Codecov deprecated the v3 uploader path; tokenless uploads are unreliable/rate-limited, and any failure is swallowed by continue-on-error, so coverage reporting may already be dead without anyone noticing.
- **Recommendation:** Either upgrade to codecov-action v5 with a documented note that cdcai must configure the Codecov app/token, or remove the step and rely on the coverage.xml artifact already uploaded at ci.yml:120-129.
- **Migration relevance:** After transfer, Codecov is tied to whichever account/app installation is configured for the repo — cdcai would need to install the Codecov app or add CODECOV_TOKEN for it to do anything. Harmless (advisory step) but it's a known-dead limb to either fix or remove before handoff.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. ci.yml:113 uses codecov/codecov-action@ab904c41d6ece82784817410c45d8b8c02684457 # v3 with continue-on-error: true (line 118) and no CODECOV_TOKEN anywhere (the only secrets referenced repo-wide are GITHUB_TOKEN). codecov-action v3 is deprecated by Codecov and tokenless v3 uploads are unreliable/rate-limited; any failure is swallowed, so coverage reporting may already be silently dead. Post-transfer, cdcai would need its own Codecov app installation or token for the step to do anything. The coverage.xml is separately preserved by the upload-artifact step at ci.yml:120-129, so removal is a viable fix. Low severity appropriate for an advisory step.

#### `ci-8` · INFO · CONFIRMED — Portability audit result: no hardcoded slugs/handles/gist-IDs/issue numbers, only secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN, GHCR image name auto-adapts; upstream cdcai has no workflows at all, so all three arrive net-new

- **Location:** .github/workflows/ (all files); scripts/ci_publish_issue.py; container-publish.yml:39,46
- **Evidence:** grep -i 'J-Schulein|schulein|jonat' over .github/ → no matches. grep 'secrets\.' → exactly three hits, all secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN (container-publish.yml:39; task-087:107,195) — zero custom secrets to migrate. container-publish.yml:46 builds the image name as `ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}`, so post-transfer it publishes to ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout automatically. ci_publish_issue.py derives repo/PR from GITHUB_REPOSITORY, GITHUB_REF (regex refs/pull/N/), and the event payload — no hardcoded issue/PR numbers or gist IDs anywhere. All 7 runs-on lines are ubuntu-latest (no self-hosted runners). GET /repos/cdcai/TowerScout/contents/.github/workflows → 'Not Found', i.e. upstream currently has no CI whatsoever. (Observed in passing, outside this dimension: README.md:22 hardcodes https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases.)
- **Recommendation:** Add a short .github/workflows/README or a handoff-doc section describing the three workflows, their triggers, and the manual container-publish release procedure (tag input + cpu/cuda121 flavor), so the new owner can operate releases without archaeology.
- **Migration relevance:** The CI is structurally clean to transfer: no secret provisioning, no slug rewrites needed inside workflows. Because upstream has never had Actions, the handoff docs should explain what each of the (post-cleanup) workflows does and that container-publish is a manual workflow_dispatch release tool; cdcai must also have Actions and GHCR packages enabled on their side (default for public repos).
- **Verifier's note:** Every claim in this portability audit reproduced: case-insensitive grep for J-Schulein/schulein/jonat over .github/ → zero matches; grep 'secrets\.' → exactly three hits, all secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN (container-publish.yml:39, task-087:107, task-087:195), so no custom secrets to migrate; container-publish.yml:46 builds `ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}` so the image name auto-adapts post-transfer; ci_publish_issue.py derives repo/PR entirely from GITHUB_REPOSITORY/GITHUB_REF (regex '^refs/pull/(\d+)/' at line 132) and the event payload with no hardcoded IDs; exactly 7 `runs-on: ubuntu-latest` lines (ci.yml x4, container-publish x1, task-087 x2), no self-hosted runners; GET /repos/cdcai/TowerScout/contents/.github/workflows → 404 Not Found, so upstream has no workflows and all three arrive net-new. The README aside also verified: README.md line 22 links https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases. Info severity and the handoff-doc recommendation are appropriate.

### 6.7 Test-suite health

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited test-suite health on branch pr-46 (HEAD 533e18b). Inventory: 334 `def test_` matches across tests/ (302 in tests/unit over 32 files, 25 in tests/integration over 5 py files, 2 in tests/backend, 1 diagnostic in tests/ root, 4 fixture false-positives in conftest.py), plus 11 JS test scripts (9 tests/frontend, 2 tests/integration) and 1 manual HTML page. CI (ci.yml) blocks only on `pytest tests/unit/` (Ubuntu, py3.11/3.12) and one JS regression test (test_task_064_provider_state_manager.js); the integration pytest step is continue-on-error. Of the 302 unit tests, 74 are permanently skipped as 'legacy/stale' and ~55 are Windows-gated and never execute on the ubuntu-only runners, so roughly 170-175 tests actually gate a merge. Things that checked out fine: the PR #46 tee/pipefail masking found in the earlier review is genuinely fixed (task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml sets `defaults: run: shell: bash`, which enables pipefail, so node exit codes now fail the job); pytest.ini is sane (120s per-test timeout, strict markers); skip reasons are explicit strings, no xfail/flaky-retry decorators anywhere in the Python suite; the simulated-helper token regex accepts the test tokens (prior Bug fixed); .agent_work UAT-contract tests run in CI because .agent_work is git-tracked; test gating via helper_available()/YOLO-metadata probes is mechanically sound. Main problems for handoff: (1) the Puppeteer workflow posts full CI logs as GitHub issues/PR comments on every run (11 bot comments on PR #46 already) and triggers on all PRs — this will spam the upstream cdcai repo after handoff; (2) everything that validates the shipped Windows package surface (bootstrap, setup, package-release.ps1, podman GPU enablement, host helper, TLS repair scripts) runs only manually on Windows and no in-repo document says so; (3) the task-087 'e2e' Puppeteer proof never executes production frontend code (IIFE-scoped builders force the tests' own fallback request-builder) — acceptable while the feature ships dark, but the workflow name oversells it. A verified-automatically-vs-manually map is included as finding tests-12.

#### `tests-1` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — CI publishes full test logs as GitHub issues/PR comments on every Puppeteer run — will spam upstream repo after handoff

- **Location:** .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:105-110,193-198 and scripts/ci_publish_issue.py
- **Evidence:** Both jobs end with an unconditional step 'Publish test logs as repo issue' running `python3 scripts/ci_publish_issue.py test-artifacts`. ci_publish_issue.py main() creates an issue titled `CI logs: {workflow} - run {run_id}` containing up to 20KB per log file; if issues are disabled (fork default) it falls back to posting the full log dump as a PR comment, then a private gist. Live proof: `curl https://api.github.com/repos/J-Schulein/TowerScout/issues/46/comments` returns 11 comments, all github-actions[bot], all starting 'Repository: J-Schulein/TowerScout Workflow: Task-087 Frontend Puppeteer Template...'. Workflow triggers are `pull_request: branches: ['**']` and `push: [feature/task-087..., main]`, so after the fork's commits land in cdcai/TowerScout (issues enabled upstream), every push to main opens a log-dump issue and every PR gets 2-3 log-dump comments.
- **Recommendation:** Before merging PR #46 / handing off: delete the two 'Publish ... as repo issue' steps and scripts/ci_publish_issue.py, or gate them with `if: failure()`. The logs are already uploaded as workflow artifacts (upload-artifact steps), so nothing is lost.
- **Migration relevance:** Directly damages a professional handoff: the new owner's issue tracker and PRs get auto-spammed with CI debug dumps that were only ever a PR-46 debugging aid.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced fully. task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml has unconditional 'Publish test logs as repo issue' steps at lines 105-110 and 193-198 (no if: condition) running scripts/ci_publish_issue.py, whose main() creates an issue titled 'CI logs: {workflow} - run {run_id}' with up to 20KB per log file, falls back to a full-dump PR comment when 'Issues has been disabled' (lines 184-199), then a gist. Live API check: GET /repos/J-Schulein/TowerScout/issues/46/comments returns exactly 11 comments, all github-actions[bot], all starting 'Repository: J-Schulein/TowerScout Workflow: Task-087 Frontend Puppeteer Template...'. Triggers confirmed: pull_request branches ['**'] and push to feature/task-087... and main (lines 3-9). Minor caveat not affecting the verdict: whether upstream actually gets issue spam depends on cdcai's GITHUB_TOKEN default permissions (read-only token would 403 silently, script still exits 0), but the behavior is proven in this repo and shipping a workflow that attempts to auto-open log-dump issues on every push to main is a genuine handoff embarrassment. High severity stands.

CONTRIBUTING.md:71 says only 'run the maintained unit-test baseline'; no doc in docs/ enumerates the Windows-only manual test matrix (docs/internal contains only codex-skills).

Verified skipif(os.name != "nt") counts: test_task_074_bootstrap 15, test_task_081_runtime_hardening 10, test_task_087_host_helper 5, test_podman_gpu_enablement 6, test_task_075_launcher_gpu 2 (= 38 markers, matching grep -rn total), plus module-level pytestmark in test_release_package_script.py:19-22 covering its 5 tests → ~43 Windows-only tests, not '~55' (the reviewer's own per-file numbers sum to 43). Also confirmed: all four ci.yml jobs are ubuntu-latest (lines 14, 40, 133, 149); 11 'PowerShell executable not found' skip guards; these are the only subprocess-invoking tests for the PS control-plane scripts; CONTRIBUTING.md says only 'run the maintained unit-test baseline'; docs/internal contains only codex-skills. Severity lowered to medium because the gap is self-announcing rather than hidden: every pytest/CI run prints the skips with explicit reasons like 'package-release.ps1 is a Windows release helper validated on Windows', so the new owner discovers it on first run; this is a documentation/knowledge-transfer item (the recommended one-paragraph testing note), not a threat to the release itself, which is certified by manual RC validation.

#### `tests-3` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Task-087 Puppeteer 'e2e' jobs never execute production frontend code — tests always take their own fallback request-builder

- **Location:** tests/frontend/test_provider_tls_live_post_poll_template.js:177-196; tests/frontend/test_provider_tls_helper_unavailable_template.js:106-122; webapp/js/towerscout.js:8281
- **Evidence:** Both templates do `typeof buildProviderTlsRepairStartRequest === 'function' ? ... : null` in page.evaluate, then fall back to constructing the POST body themselves from a test-injected payload (live_post_poll lines 181-196). The production functions `buildProviderTlsRepairStartRequest` (towerscout.js:8281) and `rememberProviderValidationResult` (towerscout.js:7997) are inside per-module IIFEs (`(function () { ... })();` wrappers throughout the bundle) and `grep -n "window.buildProviderTlsRepairStartRequest\|window.rememberProviderValidationResult" webapp/js/towerscout.js webapp/js/src/*.js` returns nothing — they are not reachable from page scope, so the fallback branch always runs. The 'helper' in the e2e job is tests/helpers/simulated_helper.js, a Node test double (started by scripts/ci_start_services.sh), not the real host-helper.ps1. Net: CI validates the request-shape contract against a simulated server; zero production JS or PowerShell is exercised. Template headers still say 'This file is a template ... will not be runnable' (live_post_poll lines 10-13) and helper_unavailable line 171 has 'TODO: Add DOM assertions'.
- **Recommendation:** Do not fix before handoff (feature ships dark); instead state plainly in the PR #46 description / handoff notes that the Puppeteer jobs verify the POST/poll contract shape against a simulated helper only, and that real browser→helper coverage requires manual Windows validation. Optionally rename the job from 'e2e' to 'contract'.
- **Migration relevance:** Prevents the new owner from over-trusting the 'Gate 3 e2e' CI signal when they later flip provider_helper_available()/PROVIDER_TLS_REPAIR_BROWSER_MUTATION_ENABLED to enable the dark-shipped control plane.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. buildProviderTlsRepairStartRequest (webapp/js/towerscout.js:8281) and rememberProviderValidationResult (:7997) both sit inside the IIFE spanning lines 7836-8750 ('(function () {' ... '})();'), and grep for window.buildProviderTlsRepairStartRequest / window.rememberProviderValidationResult across towerscout.js and webapp/js/src/*.js returns nothing — the functions are unreachable from page scope, so the templates' typeof check is always false and the test-constructed fallback body (live_post_poll lines 177-196, helper_unavailable lines 106-127) always runs. The e2e 'helper' is tests/helpers/simulated_helper.js started by scripts/ci_start_services.sh:14 (a Node double, not host-helper.ps1). Stale header 'This file is a template ... will not be runnable' confirmed at live_post_poll lines 10-13; 'TODO: Add DOM assertions' confirmed at helper_unavailable line 171. Medium is right: CI validates request-shape contract against a simulated server; zero production JS or PowerShell is exercised, and the finding correctly recommends disclosure rather than a pre-handoff fix since the feature ships dark.

#### `tests-5` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Integration pytest job is advisory (continue-on-error) AND several integration tests are structurally unable to fail

- **Location:** .github/workflows/ci.yml:110; tests/integration/test_azure_maps_integration.py:24-26; pytest.ini (addopts --disable-warnings)
- **Evidence:** ci.yml line 110: `continue-on-error: true  # Don't fail build on integration test failures initially` on the `pytest tests/integration/` step. Independently, test_azure_maps_integration.py uses the pattern `except Exception as e: print(f"... failed: {e}"); return False` instead of assertions (e.g. test_imports lines 20-36) — pytest treats a non-None return as only PytestReturnNotNoneWarning, and pytest.ini's `--disable-warnings` hides even that. Also test_azure_maps_integration.py:15 inserts `tests/webapp` (not repo webapp) on sys.path — `os.path.join(dirname(__file__), '..', 'webapp')` from tests/integration resolves to tests/webapp; imports only work because tests/conftest.py fixes sys.path.

At minimum note that 'integration tests pass' is not currently a meaningful CI statement.

- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. ci.yml:110 has 'continue-on-error: true  # Don't fail build on integration test failures initially' on the pytest tests/integration/ step. tests/integration/test_azure_maps_integration.py contains zero assert statements in its test functions — they print and 'return True/False' (test_imports lines 16-36 and the same pattern at lines 88, 131, 162, 203, 241, 273, 302), which pytest treats as pass + PytestReturnNotNoneWarning; pytest.ini addopts includes --disable-warnings, hiding that. The sys.path claim also verified: line 15 inserts os.path.join(dirname(__file__), '..', 'webapp') → tests/webapp, a directory that does not exist (ls tests/ confirms); imports work only via tests/conftest.py:45 inserting the real WEBAPP_DIR. Medium confirmed.

#### `tests-6` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — tests/backend/test_endpoint_contract.py (frontend↔Flask route contract) and tests/test_performance_metrics.py are never invoked by CI

- **Location:** .github/workflows/ci.yml:98,109 vs tests/backend/test_endpoint_contract.py:1, tests/test_performance_metrics.py:1
- **Evidence:** CI runs exactly `pytest tests/unit/ ...` (ci.yml:98) and `pytest tests/integration/ ...` (ci.yml:109). tests/backend/ (2 tests validating that all frontend CONFIG.ENDPOINTS constants exist as real Flask routes — a genuinely useful drift guard, header says 'Run After: Stage 1') and tests/test_performance_metrics.py (1 'test' that only prints diagnostics and `return`s, never asserts) are collected only by a bare local `pytest` (pytest.ini testpaths=tests) — which nobody runs in CI.
- **Recommendation:** Add tests/backend to the CI unit-test invocation (it is hermetic — imports the Flask app with mock env keys already provided by the job), and move tests/test_performance_metrics.py out of tests/ (it is a diagnostic script, not a test) or rename it so pytest doesn't collect it.
- **Migration relevance:** The endpoint-contract guard is exactly the kind of cheap regression protection the new owner would want active; currently it silently rots.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. CI's only pytest invocations are 'pytest tests/unit/ ...' (ci.yml:98) and 'pytest tests/integration/ ...' (ci.yml:109); no workflow references tests/backend or test_performance_metrics. tests/backend/test_endpoint_contract.py exists with 2 test functions containing real asserts (header confirms 'Validate frontend CONFIG.ENDPOINTS matches backend Flask routes', 'Run After: Stage 1'). tests/test_performance_metrics.py has 1 test function with zero asserts and five bare 'return' statements (lines 40, 44, 51, 94, 99) — a diagnostic script pytest collects but that can never fail meaningfully. Both are collected only by a bare local pytest (pytest.ini testpaths = tests). Medium confirmed.

#### `tests-7` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Frontend bundle-consistency guard is manual-only; CI cannot detect a stale committed towerscout.js

- **Location:** .agents/skills/towerscout-frontend-bundle-guard/scripts/check_bundle_source_consistency.py; .github/workflows/ci.yml:33-37
- **Evidence:** grep for 'check_bundle_source_consistency' and 'validate_stage_0' across .github/ returns zero hits — the guard script and Stage-0 validation exist only as the manual skill (.agents/skills/towerscout-frontend-bundle-guard/SKILL.md 'Inspect commands (read-only)') and npm script `test:stage-0`. Worse, ci.yml's frontend-test job runs `node webapp/build.js` (line 34) BEFORE the regression test, i.e. CI regenerates the bundle and tests the regenerated one — a committed webapp/js/towerscout.js that is stale relative to webapp/js/src/ passes CI undetected. Mitigation: Dockerfile lines 3-10 rebuild the bundle (`npm run build`) at image build, so the published container is safe; but the committed bundle is what a source checkout serves.
- **Recommendation:** Add one CI step after the rebuild: `git diff --exit-code -- webapp/js/towerscout.js` (fails if committed bundle didn't match sources), or run check_bundle_source_consistency.py in the frontend-test job. Cheap, high-value before handoff.
- **Migration relevance:** The generated-bundle discipline lives entirely in .agents skill files that an upstream owner using different tooling may never read; without a CI check, the first upstream contributor who edits src/ without rebuilding ships drift.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. grep for check_bundle_source_consistency and validate_stage_0 across .github/ returns zero hits (guard exists only at .agents/skills/towerscout-frontend-bundle-guard/scripts/check_bundle_source_consistency.py and npm test:stage-0). ci.yml frontend job runs 'node webapp/build.js' (line 34) before the ProviderStateManager regression test (line 37), so CI regenerates the bundle and never compares it to the committed webapp/js/towerscout.js — a stale committed bundle passes CI, and there is no 'git diff --exit-code' step. Mitigation accurately stated: Dockerfile lines 3-10 run 'npm run build' at image build so containers are safe, but a source checkout serves the committed bundle (and the task-087 puppeteer jobs serve ./webapp statically without rebuilding). Medium confirmed.

Residue verified at HEAD: unconditional 'if: always()' Diagnostics steps dumping ps aux and log heads (workflow lines 80-87, 169-178); console.log('PAGE:', ...) mirroring in both templates (live_post_poll:76-78, helper_unavailable:46); TIMEOUT=${TIMEOUT:-300} at scripts/ci_start_services.sh:23; stale 'will not be runnable' header at live_post_poll lines 10-13; helper-unavailable template runs only in the templates job (no helper), the e2e job (lines 180-185) runs only live_post_poll. #### `tests-10` · LOW · CONFIRMED — Test-double helper endpoints are compiled into the production Flask app behind TOWERSCOUT_SIMULATED_HELPER

- **Location:** webapp/towerscout.py:1902-1990
- **Evidence:** webapp/towerscout.py lines 1902-1990 define `SIMULATED_HELPER_ENABLED = os.getenv('TOWERSCOUT_SIMULATED_HELPER', ...)` and, when enabled, register `/operations/provider-tls-repair` and `/operations/<operation_id>` routes on the production app with permissive CORS (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin` echoing Origin) that fake the host-helper contract ('Simulated host helper endpoints for local testing only'). Gating is sound (default off, explicit env opt-in, logged 'TEST ONLY'), and no compose file or script in the repo sets the variable (only webapp/towerscout.py references it).
- **Recommendation:** Either remove this block before handoff (tests/helpers/simulated_helper.js already provides the same double out-of-process, and CI uses that one) or explicitly document the env var in the handoff notes so the new owner knows an env variable can add unauthenticated CORS-open mutation-shaped endpoints to the app.
- **Migration relevance:** ~90 lines of test scaffolding inside the shipped webapp is the kind of surprise a new owner should be told about, especially one adjacent to the deliberately-dark TLS-repair control plane.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. webapp/towerscout.py lines ~1902-1990 define SIMULATED_HELPER_ENABLED from env var TOWERSCOUT_SIMULATED_HELPER and conditionally register /operations/provider-tls-repair (POST/OPTIONS) and /operations/<operation_id> (GET/OPTIONS) routes with Access-Control-Allow-Origin echoing the request Origin, faking the host-helper contract ('Simulated host helper endpoints for local testing only', logged as 'TEST ONLY'). Gating assessment accurate: default off, explicit opt-in, and a repo-wide grep shows webapp/towerscout.py is the ONLY file referencing the variable (no compose file or script sets it; CI uses the out-of-process tests/helpers/simulated_helper.js instead). Low severity with document-or-remove recommendation is correct.

- **Recommendation:** No action needed on gating mechanics; the coverage consequences are covered by tests-2/tests-3/tests-8.
- **Migration relevance:** Positive signal worth passing on: the suite fails loudly rather than hanging, and skips are self-describing.
- **Verifier's note:** Positive/info finding reproduced by spot checks: tests/integration/test_provider_tls_server_side_template.py gates both tests on a live probe of TEST_HELPER_BASE_URL via @pytest.mark.skipif(not helper_available(), reason='Host helper not reachable on TEST_HELPER_BASE_URL') (line 41 area); tests/integration/test_end_to_end.py skips at lines 95-102 when no YOLO engine metadata or model weights exist; grep across tests/ for xfail/flaky/reruns returns zero matches; pytest.ini sets timeout = 120 with timeout_method = thread. Gating mechanics are sound as claimed.

#### `tests-12` · INFO · CONFIRMED — Map: what is verified automatically vs only manually (handoff reference)

- **Location:** .github/workflows/ci.yml; .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml; tests/
- **Evidence:** VERIFIED AUTOMATICALLY, MERGE-BLOCKING (ubuntu, py3.11+3.12): ~170-175 unit tests — Flask routes/config/geocoding/provider-HTTP/device-policy/YOLO-loader contract/sanitization/license-notices/release-manifest schema, plus static source-text contracts over the PowerShell scripts and workflows (test_import_assets_script.py, test_runtime_hardening.py, test_container_publish_workflow.py, test_frontend_provider_tls.py — these grep .ps1/.js text, they do not execute the scripts); flake8 E9/F63/F7/F82; ProviderStateManager JS regression; task-087 POST/poll request-shape vs the Node simulated helper (with pipefail active since 533e18b, so failures are real). ADVISORY IN CI (cannot block): pytest tests/integration (ci.yml:110), black, mypy, bandit, Docker image build, Trivy. NEVER RUN IN CI (manual only): all PowerShell behavioral tests (~55 tests, Windows-only — bootstrap/setup, package-release.ps1, enable-podman-gpu.ps1, launcher hardening, host-helper.ps1); package generation and asset-bundle verification; TLS repair scripts' runtime behavior (repair-provider-tls.ps1 / import-tls-ca.ps1 have only source-text asserts); install-podman-compose-provider (source-text asserts only, in Windows-gated test_task_081_runtime_hardening.py); detection browser smoke with live Google/Azure keys (npm run test:browser:detect); Stage-0 bundle validation (validate_stage_0.sh); bundle-consistency guard (.agents skill script); tests/backend endpoint contract; live-provider end-to-end (validated via the manual UAT/RC process recorded outside the repo in Validation Evidence). Two precision notes that do not change the info-level map: (1) pipefail on the tee'd test steps comes from the 'defaults: run: shell: bash' block added in 2b67123 (the parent of 533e18b) — so it is active at HEAD as claimed, just introduced one commit earlier; (2) the actually-gating unit count is ~185 by def-test arithmetic (302 - 74 legacy - 43 Windows-gated) rather than '~170-175', a minor undercount. Recommendation to paste the map into handoff notes is sound.

### 6.8 Documentation & licensing

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Docs audit of the TowerScout fork (branch pr-46, HEAD 533e18b) covering README, the docs/ tree (23 files incl. internal/legacy/release/support subdirs), CONTRIBUTING, the 8-file license/provenance set, .github agent-instruction files, release-manifest.v1.json, and a repo-wide J-Schulein grep, cross-checked against the live GitHub API (fork parent confirmed as cdcai/TowerScout; rc7.1 release assets enumerated).

What is healthy: the core end-user documentation is in genuinely good shape and current. docs/quick-start.md, docs/user-guide.md, docs/package-guide.md, docs/project-overview.md and the four new runtime guides all carry 'Applies to: ... RC7 provider TLS repair baseline / Last reviewed 2026-06-29' headers, accurately describe the current setup path (setup-towerscout.cmd, start.bat -Engine/-Gpu, stop/status/logs/import-assets helpers, repair-provider-tls.cmd, install-podman-compose-provider.cmd -Apply), and cover Docker and Podman, CPU and GPU. The md/html twins that exist are in sync. Old rc1-era filenames were properly converted to redirect stubs rather than left stale. The license/provenance set (LICENSE, NOTICE, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES, MODEL_LICENSES, DATA_LICENSES, PROVIDER_TERMS, SOURCE.txt, SBOM.txt, docs/legacy/LEGACY-LICENSE-NOTICE.md) is internally consistent about the agpl-yolo composite posture, and THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES matches webapp/requirements.txt (torch/torchvision/ultralytics/efficientnet_pytorch all present and pinned). release-manifest.v1.json is a template by design (release_version: 'template'); the generated control ZIP is documented as authoritative, which is consistent across NOTICE/SOURCE/SBOM, and its schema is unit-tested. CONTRIBUTING and .github/instructions/github-repo-management.instructions.md match observed repo behavior (branch names, squash merges, PR template). scripts/start.cmd etc. referenced by docs all exist — no references to removed scripts were found.

The problems cluster around handoff/migration rather than content accuracy: (1) 20 tracked non-archive files hardcode J-Schulein GitHub/GHCR URLs (README, every user guide's release URL, compose.yaml/.env.example/package-release.ps1 image defaults, plus 5 unit tests asserting the namespace) — the single biggest pre-handoff work item; (2) no file anywhere states the repo is a fork of cdcai/TowerScout; (3) .github/copilot-instructions.md is frozen at 2026-05-27 RC1-era status and will misdirect the new owner's agents; (4) the Task-087 host-helper scripts ship in the release package with zero docs describing the feature or its dark state; (5) the self-imposed per-release SBOM requirement has never been met (rc7.1 assets contain no SBOM). Lower-severity items: two broken/stale cross-references (DATA_LICENSES.md and oci-runtime-contract.md), LICENSE being a posture narrative (NOASSERTION on GitHub — deliberate but transfers an open legal question), missing concrete test commands in CONTRIBUTING, Codex-specific internal tooling docs, and a stale local v0.1.0 tag that collides with the likely stable-release name.

#### `docs-1` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Hardcoded J-Schulein GitHub and GHCR references in 20 tracked non-archive files (47 total incl. .agent_work) break or mislead after migration to cdcai

- **Location:** README.md:22; compose.yaml:3; .env.example:5; docs/* (9 files); scripts/package-release.ps1:37; scripts/lib/TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117; tests/unit/* (5 files)
- **Evidence:** git grep -rni 'J-Schulein' -- . ':(exclude).agent_work' hits 20 files. User-facing release URLs: README.md:22 '[Releases](https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases)'; docs/quick-start.md:149, docs/quick-start.html:71, docs/package-guide.md:142, docs/docker-cpu-user-guide.md:64, docs/docker-gpu-user-guide.md:73, docs/podman-cpu-user-guide.md:86, docs/podman-gpu-user-guide.md:108 all print https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases. GHCR image namespace: compose.yaml:3 'image: ${TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE:-ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:latest-cpu}', .env.example:5, scripts/package-release.ps1:37 default Image, scripts/lib/TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117 fallback, docs/support/oci-runtime-contract.md:137 'Current repository package target: ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout', docs/support/oci-quick-start.md:117-139, docs/release/release-asset-bundle-contract.md:44, docs/package-guide.md:288. Five test files assert the j-schulein image string (e.g. tests/unit/test_task_081_runtime_hardening.py:61-65). git grep -rli over all tracked files counts 47 files, 27 under .agent_work.
- **Recommendation:** Decide the post-handoff release/registry namespace with cdcai before 2026-07-10. Either (a) do a coordinated rename pass: github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout -> github.com/cdcai/TowerScout and ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout -> ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout in the 15 non-test files plus the 5 test assertions (they will fail otherwise), and republish/retag images; or (b) document explicitly in README and package-guide that release assets and images remain on the fork for the pilot generation. The .agent_work hits are historical records and can stay as-is.
- **Migration relevance:** After handoff, releases/images will live under cdcai (or wherever cdcai republishes). Every pilot-user doc points users at the J-Schulein fork's Releases page, and the default Compose/packaging image is ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout. If the fork is archived or images are republished, these references silently point at stale artifacts.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced fully: git grep counts 47 tracked files with J-Schulein (27 in .agent_work, 20 outside). Spot-checked every cited location — README.md:22 releases URL; the 7 docs files printing https://github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases (quick-start.md:149, quick-start.html:71, package-guide.md:142, docker-cpu:64, docker-gpu:73, podman-cpu:86, podman-gpu:108); ghcr.io/j-schulein defaults in compose.yaml:3, .env.example:5, package-release.ps1:37, TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117; contract docs (oci-runtime-contract.md:131,137; oci-quick-start.md:117-139; release-asset-bundle-contract.md:44; package-guide.md:288); and 5 test files asserting the j-schulein image string (test_task_081_runtime_hardening.py:61-65,687-735 plus 4 others). All exact. GHCR user-namespace packages do not transfer with a repo handoff and the fork's Releases page is the only download path in every pilot doc, so this genuinely needs a namespace decision before the stable release/handoff. Severity high is warranted.

The only prose describing it lives in .agent_work/tasks/active/TASK-087-host-side-tls-repair-control-plane.md (internal task tracker). The feature is intentionally dark (webapp/ts_provider_http.py:120 provider_helper_available hardcoded; webapp/js/src/setup-wizard.js:17 PROVIDER_TLS_REPAIR_BROWSER_MUTATION_ENABLED = false), and neither the dark state nor the enable path is documented in any doc a new owner would read.
- **Recommendation:** Add a short docs/support/host-helper.md (or a section in docs/support/oci-runtime-contract.md): what the helper is, that provider_helper_available() and the frontend flag are hardcoded off in the shipped build, the two source locations of the gates, the security posture, and what Gate 3+ enabling would require. Link it from the PR #46 description and the handoff notes.
- **Migration relevance:** The new owner inherits ~2,300+ lines of PowerShell control-plane code that ships in every release package with no explanation of what it is, that it is deliberately disabled, or how/whether to enable it. This is exactly the kind of undocumented dark surface that erodes trust in a handoff.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced: zero matches for 'host helper'/'host-helper' in docs/, README.md, CONTRIBUTING.md. The four helper files exist (2,559 lines total; TowerScoutHostHelper.ps1 alone is 2,495, so the '~2,300+' claim is accurate) and scripts/package-release.ps1 stages host-helper.cmd/.ps1/-visible.cmd into every package. Dark gates verified at webapp/ts_provider_http.py:120-121 (provider_helper_available returns False) and webapp/js/src/setup-wizard.js:17 (PROVIDER_TLS_REPAIR_BROWSER_MUTATION_ENABLED = false). Aggravating detail found during verification: docs/package-guide.md:818-820 still says the one-click browser repair helper 'is not part of the rc7 package baseline' even though PR #46 now ships the helper scripts in the package — the only doc sentence touching the topic is now contradictory. Medium appropriate.

#### `docs-5` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — SBOM requirement is self-declared but never fulfilled: SBOM.txt is a placeholder and no release (incl. rc7.1) attaches a generated SBOM

- **Location:** SBOM.txt:1-19; release-manifest.v1.json:61-64; scripts/package-release.ps1:438-452

It is not the generated SBOM itself.' release-manifest.v1.json:63: '"status": "Release SBOM must be generated or attached for each release candidate."'. scripts/package-release.ps1:438-452 writes the same placeholder text into every generated package ('This file is a release-package reference, not the generated SBOM itself'). GitHub API asset list for v0.1.0-rc7.1 shows only 6 assets (cpu.zip, cuda121.zip, assets zip, and three .sha256 sidecars) — no SBOM artifact. THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md:36-37 similarly defers: 'Release maintainers should generate or attach an SBOM for the exact package/image release.'
- **Recommendation:** For the stable release, either generate a real SBOM (e.g. syft/trivy against the pinned image digest plus webapp/requirements.txt and package-lock.json) and attach it to the release, or amend SBOM.txt / release-manifest sbom.status wording to an honest deferred posture the new owner explicitly accepts.
- **Migration relevance:** The compliance file set repeatedly promises an SBOM per release candidate; seven RCs later none exists. For the final stable release handed to a CDC-affiliated owner, an unmet self-imposed compliance requirement is a visible gap (the AGPL corresponding-source posture otherwise looks carefully done).
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced: SBOM.txt:5-6 states it is not the generated SBOM; release-manifest.v1.json sbom.status = 'Release SBOM must be generated or attached for each release candidate'; package-release.ps1 (~lines 437-452) writes the same placeholder into every package; THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md defers to release maintainers. GitHub API for v0.1.0-rc7.1 lists exactly 6 assets (cpu.zip, cuda121.zip, assets zip + three .sha256 sidecars) — no SBOM artifact. Self-declared requirement unmet across all RCs. Medium appropriate.

#### `docs-9` · LOW · CONFIRMED — In-repo end-user guides exist in overlapping triplicate (md + generated html + four runtime-specific guides) — drift risk with the out-of-repo Setup-and-User-Guides folder

- **Location:** docs/quick-start.{md,html}, docs/user-guide.{md,html}, docs/project-overview.{md,html}, docs/docker-*-user-guide.md, docs/podman-*-user-guide.md, docs/package-guide.md
- **Evidence:** docs/ contains hand-synced md/html pairs (all currently aligned at 'Last reviewed 2026-06-29': quick-start.html, user-guide.html, project-overview.html match their .md headers; verified via grep of 'Last reviewed'). PR #46 adds four new runtime guides (git diff main...pr-46 --stat: docker-cpu +232, docker-gpu +270, podman-cpu +286, podman-gpu +325 lines) that repeat setup/start/TLS content also present in quick-start.md and package-guide.md — e.g. install-podman-compose-provider.cmd appears in package-guide.md:723, podman-cpu-user-guide.md:71,206, podman-gpu-user-guide.md:77,238. The four new guides exist only as .md with no .html twin, so the html set is now an incomplete mirror. Redirect stubs (docs/v1-rc1-quick-start.md, docs/v1-rc1-package-guide.md, docs/towerscout-user-guide.md and 17-line html stubs) are intentional and fine.
- **Recommendation:** In the handoff notes, declare docs/*.md in this repo canonical, state that the .html files are derived (and how they are regenerated), and either regenerate/retire the html mirror or add html twins for the four runtime guides before the stable release.
- **Migration relevance:** Any copies in the out-of-repo Setup-and-User-Guides folder make a third copy of the same instructions. Without a declared canonical source, the new owner will not know which of md/html/external-copy wins when they diverge (the html set is already structurally behind: no runtime-guide html twins).
- **Verifier's note:** In-repo facts all reproduce: quick-start/user-guide/project-overview md+html pairs each stamped 'Last reviewed 2026-06-29'; the four PR-46 runtime guides (git diff main...HEAD --stat: +232/+270/+286/+325 lines, matching exactly) have no .html twins, so the html mirror is structurally incomplete; duplicated setup content confirmed (install-podman-compose-provider.cmd at package-guide.md:723, podman-cpu:71,206, podman-gpu:77,238). The out-of-repo Setup-and-User-Guides folder cannot be verified from the repo, but the finding's severity does not rest on it. Low appropriate.

#### `docs-12` · LOW · ADJUSTED — Stale local tag v0.1.0 (2025-12-04, 'Project Status: 15% complete') collides with the likely stable-release name

- **Location:** tag v0.1.0 -> commit e78974d (2025-12-04)
- **Evidence:** git show v0.1.0 --no-patch: annotated tag on e78974d6, dated 2025-12-04, message 'TowerScout v0.1.0: Security Foundation Complete ... Project Status: 15% complete'. GitHub API releases/tags/v0.1.0 -> Not Found (no release object). All nine v0.1.0-rc* tags are newer than it.
- **Recommendation:** Before publishing the stable release, verify whether v0.1.0 exists on origin; pick a stable tag name that does not collide (e.g. v0.1.0-ga or v0.1.1) or coordinate deliberate re-tagging with the owner. Do not let release docs cite bare 'v0.1.0' until resolved.
- **Migration relevance:** Primarily a release/tag-hygiene issue (likely another reviewer's dimension), but it directly affects docs: if the stable release is tagged v0.1.0, the tag already points to an 8-month-old commit, and any doc/release URL referencing v0.1.0 would resolve to the wrong source ref.
- **Verifier's note:** Kernel confirmed but the claim understates scope: the tag is not merely local. git show v0.1.0 confirms annotated tag on e78974d6, dated 2025-12-04, message includes 'Project Status: 15% complete'; all nine v0.1.0-rc* tags are newer; GitHub API releases/tags/v0.1.0 returns 404 (no release object). However, git ls-remote origin shows refs/tags/v0.1.0 EXISTS ON ORIGIN (f00e870c, peeled to e78974d6), so the collision is live on the shared remote: publishing a GitHub release named v0.1.0 would silently attach to the 8-month-old 15%-complete commit unless the tag is deliberately moved first. Corrected claim: 'Stale tag v0.1.0 exists on both local and origin and collides with the likely stable-release name; the finding's own recommendation step (verify origin) resolves to yes.' Severity stays low for the docs dimension (it is primarily release-hygiene), but the recommendation to avoid bare v0.1.0 or coordinate re-tagging is validated and slightly more urgent than the original wording implies.

Info severity appropriate.

### 6.9 .agent_work / .agents workspace

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited .agent_work/ (5.8 MB, 304 files on disk; 290 git-tracked, plus untracked evidence/ and git-ignored pytest-temp/) and .agents/ (104 KB, 33 files, all tracked) at HEAD 533e18b. Structure is disciplined and self-consistent: tasks/active contains exactly the genuinely-active TASK-087 (Gate 3 = PR #46), 64 completed task records, 15 ADRs, sprint retrospectives, a 17-item forward backlog, and a working validator — `python .agent_work/scripts/validate_agent_work.py` passes (exit 0), all 12 .agents/skills have complete SKILL.md/openai.yaml/scripts and the skill-router table matches the directory set exactly. Things checked that turned out FINE: no full API keys anywhere (regex sweep for AIza/sk-/ghp_/AKIA found only the dummy example key), the March 2026 BFG history cleanup held (old exposed key survives in history only as a 15-char prefix quoted inside the cleanup report itself), no private AOI coordinates, no screenshots or binaries, the ignored pytest-temp .env is empty, and the untracked evidence file is a benign 367-byte version manifest. Issues found are all modest and fixable in one small commit: a recorded local TLS-inspection CA name+thumbprint in completed-task files in violation of the project's own later evidence policy; two truncated (unusable) Google key prefixes; CDC staff emails in the pilot packet (intentional, handoff target is CDC itself); machine paths for prior users bg/iek; a misplaced untracked evidence file; and TASK-087's active status needing finalization on the last commit. The biggest handoff-continuity gap is that ~17 future-work items (including the AGPL-exit task TASK-078 and key-policy task TASK-076) exist only in task-backlog.md and should become GitHub issues. Disposition recommendation: KEEP both directories in-repo — they are the only durable record of six sprints of rationale and validation evidence, upstream cdcai's repo layout is entirely different anyway so nothing about the fork is 'conventional' for them, and relocation would break internal links and the validators' hardcoded paths — but apply the small redaction commit, resolve the stray evidence file, finalize TASK-087 state, export the backlog to issues, and add a README/HANDOFF pointer explaining the directories to the new owner.

- **Recommendation:** As part of the final handoff commit: update TASK-087 status to reflect its actual end state (e.g., 'Gate 3 merged dark in PR #46; Gate 4 managed-network validation not performed — see issue #X'), refresh current-tasks.md, and rerun validate_agent_work.py (it enforces active/ vs current-tasks.md consistency).
- **Migration relevance:** If PR #46 merges before handoff, an 'IN_PROGRESS' active task plus Gate-4 'blocked' language will confuse the new owner about whether the dark-shipped helper is done. The final commit should tell the truth about where Sprint 07 stopped.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. .agent_work/tasks/active/ contains only TASK-087 (md + evidence dir), header status 'IN_PROGRESS - Gate 3 product integration proof started after PR #45 helper proof merge' — matches reality (draft PR #46 open at 533e18b). tasks/completed/ has exactly 64 entries. Staleness confirmed: the single context/status doc is TASK-087-PR46-97b2d9a-start-contract-review.md, pinned to commit 97b2d9a while PR HEAD is 533e18b; current-tasks.md last committed 2026-07-06 10:08 (b288d75) while the quality-review fix commits 2b67123/533e18b landed 2026-07-07 17:54/18:02. Ran python .agent_work/scripts/validate_agent_work.py — 'passed', exit 0, and the script does enforce active/ vs current-tasks.md consistency (it has no write operations, verified before running). Low severity and the finalize-on-last-commit recommendation are sound.

#### `agent-work-8` · INFO · CONFIRMED — Disposition: keep .agent_work/ and .agents/ in-repo as-is, with a pointer doc; do not relocate or prune

- **Location:** .agent_work/ (5.8 MB, 304 files, 290 tracked), .agents/ (104 KB, 33 files, all tracked)
- **Evidence:** Sizes: tasks/completed 2.4 MB (64 task records incl. validation evidence), context 2.4 MB (of which archive/ 1.9 MB), user-testing 594 KB (19 UT issue analyses), decisions/ 164 KB (15 ADRs), design.md 45 KB, requirements.md 28 KB. Zero binaries (no .png/.jpg/.zip/.pt found). Upstream check: `curl api.github.com/repos/cdcai/TowerScout/contents/` shows upstream root has only Model/, SyntheticData/, TowerScoutSite/, hosting/, webapp/ — the fork's entire structure (including these dirs) is new to cdcai, so .agent_work is no more 'foreign' than the rest of the handoff.
- **Recommendation:** Keep both directories in place. Before handoff: (1) apply the small redaction commit from agent-work-1/-4; (2) resolve the untracked evidence file (agent-work-6); (3) finalize TASK-087/current-tasks state (agent-work-5); (4) add a short section to the top-level README or a HANDOFF.md explaining that .agent_work/ is the engineering-history/task system (entry points: README.md, current-tasks.md, task-backlog.md, completed-tasks.md, decisions/) and .agents/skills/ are reusable review checklists — so cdcai reads them as an asset, not cruft; (5) export the backlog to GitHub issues (agent-work-2).
- **Migration relevance:** These directories are the ONLY durable record of 6 sprints of rationale: the AGPL/YOLO licensing strategy (TASK-069), the TLS/managed-network saga (TASK-025/086/087), all RC validation evidence, retrospectives, and the backlog. Deleting them destroys institutional memory with no privacy gain (content stays in git history regardless); moving 300+ files to docs/engineering-history would break internal relative links and the validators' hardcoded ROOT/.agent_work paths for purely cosmetic benefit.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced the factual basis. Sizes: .agent_work 5.8M / 304 files / 290 git-tracked; .agents 104K / 33 files / 33 tracked; tasks/completed 2.4M; context 2.4M (archive 1.9M); user-testing 594K; decisions 164K; design.md 45,281 bytes; requirements.md 28,496 bytes. Zero binaries (find for png/jpg/jpeg/zip/pt/gif = 0). Upstream check reproduced: cdcai/TowerScout root contains only Model, SyntheticData, TowerScoutSite, hosting, webapp (+ README.md, LICENSE.TXT, .gitignore, .DS_Store), so the fork's structure is entirely new to upstream. The 'would break validators' claim verified: validate_agent_work.py line 9 hardcodes AGENT_WORK = ROOT / '.agent_work', and the hygiene skill's check_agent_work_quick.py resolves the same path. Disposition recommendation (keep in place, redact, pointer doc, export backlog) is consistent with findings 1-6 and is a judgment call, not a factual claim; info severity correct.

### 6.10 Repo structure

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited all top-level entries of the TowerScout fork (branch pr-46, HEAD 533e18b) for handoff readiness. Overall structure is healthy: (1) dist/ is NOT committed — `git ls-files dist/` returns 0 files and `git log --all -- dist/` is empty; it is gitignored (`dist/` in .gitignore) and is only the default `-OutputDir` of scripts/package-release.ps1 (local copy holds stale June-15 rc5 zips, harmless). (2) No model weights are in git: .gitignore blocks `*.pt/*.pth/*.onnx/model_params/`, and the only tracked binary found repo-wide (zip/pt/log/docx/pkl scan) is one 551KB UAT .docx under .agent_work/. (3) Model/, SyntheticData/, TowerScoutSite/ (107 files), and hosting/ are all inherited upstream cdcai/TowerScout content, byte-identical sizes to upstream (verified Model/ via GitHub API: e.g. w210_model_NB.ipynb 25,939,343 bytes both sides) and untouched since the initial commits — they should stay for upstream parity; total pack size is only 31.23 MiB so clone weight is a non-issue. (4) SOURCE.txt, SBOM.txt, and root release-manifest.v1.json are intentional self-describing templates ("release_version": "template"); release-specific versions are generated by scripts/package-release.ps1 (lines 435/452/524) — current and accurate. (5) docs/ is current: all main guides stamped "Last reviewed: 2026-06-29"; towerscout-user-guide.md and v1-rc1-* files are deliberate 400-byte redirect stubs kept so old links don't 404. (6) All 4 compose*.yaml files are referenced (TowerScoutCompose.ps1, package-release.ps1, docs). (7) Entry points are coherent, not duplicative: setup-towerscout.cmd (first-run, delegates to bootstrap.ps1), start.bat (launch.ps1), bootstrap.cmd (direct validated path); the first two are well documented in package-guide.md/quick-start.md and runtime guides. (8) .env was never committed (`git log --all -- .env` and `-- "*.env"` empty). Remaining items are decision/hygiene findings below — the biggest is whether ~330 fork-internal agent-workflow files (.agent_work/, .agents/, docs/internal/codex-skills, .github/copilot-instructions.md + instructions/) should ship to cdcai. Nothing found rises above medium severity; no blockers for the stable release.

#### `structure-1` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Fork-internal agent workspace (~330 files, ~5.2MB) is tracked and will transfer to cdcai as-is

- **Location:** .agent_work/ (290 tracked files), .agents/ (33 tracked files), docs/internal/codex-skills/ (3 files)
- **Evidence:** `git ls-files .agent_work/ | wc -l` = 290 (~5,206,285 bytes total via cat-file, incl. binary .agent_work/user-testing/instructions/TowerScout_V1_RC1_UAT_User_Guide.docx ~551KB — the only tracked binary of its kind repo-wide); `git ls-files .agents/ | wc -l` = 33 (OpenAI/Codex agent skills, e.g. .agents/skills/towerscout-agent-work-hygiene/SKILL.md); docs/internal/codex-skills/README.md: "TowerScout Codex Skills / Updated package generated 2026-05-14 ... copy the .agents directory into the TowerScout repository root". Content is sprint trackers, task archives (tasks/completed/TASK-038...), retrospectives, ADRs, design-doc backups (e.g. design-task-038-revised-v2.3-backup.md).
- **Recommendation:** Make an explicit keep/exclude decision before 2026-07-10. Recommended: keep .agent_work/ (it documents why decisions were made and the README explains it) but say so in the handoff notes; prune obvious dead weight (the .docx binary, tasks/completed design-doc *-backup.md quadruplicates). .agents/ and docs/internal/codex-skills are agent-tooling specific — keep only if cdcai will use the same agent workflow, otherwise delete in one commit.
- **Migration relevance:** This is the single largest structural decision for the handoff: cdcai receives the fork team's entire internal project-management and AI-agent tooling history. It is self-describing (.agent_work/README.md explains the layout) and arguably valuable context, but it is unmistakably internal working state, not product.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced: `git ls-files` = 290 (.agent_work/), 33 (.agents/), 3 (docs/internal/codex-skills/); summed blob size of .agent_work/ = 5,206,285 bytes exactly; docs/internal/codex-skills/README.md says 'Updated package generated 2026-05-14' and instructs copying .agents into repo root; TASK-038 design backups exist (six, v2.0-v2.5, at .agent_work/context/archive/2026-02/Task-038 Technical Design Archive/); TowerScout_V1_RC1_UAT_User_Guide.docx is the only tracked .docx/office binary repo-wide (`git ls-files | grep -iE '\.(docx|xlsx|pptx|pdf|zip|exe|dll|bin)$'` returns only it). Two evidence nits that do not change the finding: the docx blob is 413,522 bytes (~404KB), not ~551KB, and the backups are sextuplicates not quadruplicates. Medium severity is appropriate — this is a genuine keep/exclude decision for the handoff.

#### `structure-8` · INFO · REFUTED — bootstrap.cmd's direct-use role is never documented (appears only in layout listings)

- **Location:** bootstrap.cmd (root); docs/package-guide.md:262; docs/quick-start.md:253
- **Evidence:** Three top-level entry points exist: setup-towerscout.cmd → scripts/setup-towerscout.ps1 (which resolves asset/package ZIPs then delegates to bootstrap.ps1), start.bat → scripts/launch.ps1, bootstrap.cmd → scripts/bootstrap.ps1. Docs explain setup-towerscout.cmd ("Guided Setup Path ... delegates to the validated bootstrap path", package-guide.md:300-323) and start.bat (all four runtime guides), but bootstrap.cmd is only listed in the two package-layout code blocks with no usage guidance.
- **Recommendation:** Add one sentence to docs/package-guide.md's layout section: bootstrap.cmd is the lower-level validated bring-up that setup-towerscout.cmd delegates to; use it directly only when re-running bring-up with explicit flags after first setup.
- **Migration relevance:** Minor: a new owner or support engineer seeing three launchers at package root cannot tell from the docs when to invoke bootstrap.cmd directly. The architecture itself is sound, not duplicative.
- **Verifier's note:** The core claim 'bootstrap.cmd's direct-use role is never documented (appears only in layout listings)' is factually wrong. docs/release/release-asset-bundle-contract.md:115 explicitly documents it with usage syntax: '`bootstrap.cmd -AssetZip <asset-zip>` remains the explicit-path support helper behind that setup flow', and docs/support/oci-runtime-contract.md:119 lists 'top-level `bootstrap.cmd` support helper for explicit artifact paths and advanced validation'. It also appears in docs/support/oci-quick-start.md:63 and docs/quick-start.html:85 beyond the two layout blocks the finding cites. True that the two primary end-user guides (package-guide.md, quick-start.md) only show it in layout listings, but the direct-use role IS documented in the support/release contract docs where a support engineer would look; at info severity this residual gap is a non-issue for the handoff.

- **Migration relevance:** These are the directories most likely to be misjudged as fork cruft; they are upstream heritage, and deleting them would create pointless divergence when handing the fork back to cdcai.
- **Verifier's note:** Every checkable claim reproduced: dist/ has 0 tracked files, empty `git log --all -- dist/`, .gitignore line 7 = `dist/`, local dir holds only Jun 15 rc5-candidate zips (109,803 B + .sha256); Model/ has exactly 9 tracked files and the three large notebooks are blob-SHA-identical to upstream cdcai/TowerScout main via GitHub API (EN_classifier_train.ipynb e174a7c3 / 24,515,386 B; w210_model_NB.ipynb e9e69f8d / 25,939,343 B; w210_model_NB_v2.ipynb d1943e65 / 25,675,741 B — local `git ls-files -s` hashes match exactly); the extensionless EN_classifier_train was renamed to .ipynb in upstream commit ff233e0 ('EN extension edit', Karen Wong, 2021-04-15), so the 24MB history blob predates the fork; hosting/ last touched by 0a8f441 'hosting new repo on AWS' and 4c470a8 'Initial commit, license header' (2021, gunnarmein-ucbischool); TowerScoutSite/ = 107 tracked files incl. assets/img/features-2.gif at 8,606,341 B, exists upstream (API 200, as do hosting/ and SyntheticData/), and .dockerignore line 61 is exactly `TowerScoutSite/`; `git count-objects -vH` size-pack = 31.23 MiB. Correct no-action conclusion — deleting these would create pointless divergence from upstream.

### 6.11 Dead code & orphans

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Dead-code sweep of the TowerScout repo at pr-46 HEAD 533e18b (excluding already-fixed F9/F10 items). What was checked and found CLEAN: (1) webapp Python import graph — all 24 ts_*.py modules plus ts_yolov5_local and vendor are reachable from towerscout.py (grep-built graph: ts_assets via ts_runtime, ts_tls via ts_config/ts_provider_http/ts_geocoding, everything else imported directly); no orphaned webapp module. (2) JS bundle — all 30 files under webapp/js/src appear in build.js MODULE_ORDER (30/30 match vs git ls-files); jquery-3.5.1.min.js and azure_maps_debug.js are loaded by templates/towerscout.html (:542 and dynamic ?debug=azure loader :676-688); the audit-era strays (towerscout.js.stage3.bak, comment_providers.sh, temp_extract_providers.sh) are already gone. (3) compose.build.yaml is ALIVE (scripts/lib/TowerScoutCompose.ps1:1486 and docs/support/oci-quick-start.md:208); compose.gpu.yaml/compose.gpu.podman.yaml widely referenced. (4) requirements-dev.txt is ALIVE (ci.yml:63). (5) scripts/ci_publish_issue.py and ci_start_services.sh ARE referenced by task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml at HEAD — not dead, but the issue-publisher is debug scaffolding flagged as finding dead-code-1. (6) All six scripts/lib modules and every script in the package-release.ps1 $releaseFiles list are referenced/packaged. (7) tests/unit/release_manifest_contract.py is intentionally non-test-named and imported by test_release_manifest_schema.py:6 and test_release_package_script.py:10 — not dead. (8) Windows-only skipif marks (test_task_074/075/081/087, test_podman_gpu_enablement) and helper_available skipifs are legitimate conditional skips. (9) hosting/, Model/, SyntheticData/, TowerScoutSite/ are upstream-heritage (initial/early upstream commits) — left alone. (10) dist/ and webapp/__pycache__ are untracked. FINDINGS (11): the highest-value items are the still-live CI step that files a GitHub issue with logs on every workflow run (will spam the handed-off repo), the /debug-azure-maps route serving a file that has never existed in the repo, and four permanently-skipped legacy test modules whose own skip text says they were replaced by TASK-052. The rest is low-severity fork-era residue: the vestigial stage-0 validation harness (confirming the structure reviewer's validate_stage_0.sh suspicion — technically referenced by package.json but functionally dead), an orphaned TASK-052 container smoke script, phase1_validation.js, the 176KB towerscout.original.js backup that ships in the Docker image, an orphaned TASK-041 stress test, two commented-out-only templates plus a dead CDC IP-allowlist block, five unwired-but-plausibly-useful frontend contract tests, and two completed manual-verification checklists. Nothing found blocks the stable release; all findings are cleanup items that make the 2026-07-10 handoff cleaner.

#### `dead-code-1` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — task-087 workflow auto-files a GitHub issue with test logs on every run (mid-PR-46 debug scaffolding still live)

- **Location:** .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml:110 and :198 (plus scripts/ci_publish_issue.py)
- **Evidence:** Workflow steps 'Publish test logs as repo issue' (line ~105-110) and 'Publish e2e logs as repo issue' (line ~193-198) both run `python3 scripts/ci_publish_issue.py test-artifacts` with GITHUB_TOKEN and have NO `if:` condition (grep -n "if:" on the workflow shows only lines 81 and 170, which are the upload-artifact steps' `if: always()`). Workflow triggers: `on: pull_request: branches: ['**']` and `push` to feature/task-087-gate3-product-integration and main. scripts/ci_publish_issue.py builds an issue body from GITHUB_REPOSITORY/RUN_ID env and POSTs via the API. The same logs are already preserved by the preceding actions/upload-artifact steps.
- **Recommendation:** Before merging PR #46 / handoff, delete the two 'Publish ... logs as repo issue' steps from the workflow and remove scripts/ci_publish_issue.py (or gate the steps behind `if: failure()` + workflow_dispatch only if the team wants to keep them through 2026-07-15).
- **Migration relevance:** This is not dead code today (it IS referenced at HEAD), but it is debugging machinery added mid-PR-46: after merge, every PR and every push to main in the handed-off cdcai/TowerScout repo would auto-create a GitHub issue containing CI logs — issue-tracker spam in the new owner's repo. Artifacts are redundant with upload-artifact.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced. Workflow .github/workflows/task-087-frontend-puppeteer.yml has 'Publish test logs as repo issue' (lines 105-110) and 'Publish e2e logs as repo issue' (lines 193-198), both running scripts/ci_publish_issue.py with GITHUB_TOKEN and no `if:` (only lines 81/170 have if: always(), and those are actually the Diagnostics steps, not upload-artifact — immaterial slip; publish steps run under default if: success()). Triggers confirmed: pull_request branches ['**'] plus push to the feature branch and main. Mechanism correction: GitHub API shows has_issues=false on BOTH J-Schulein/TowerScout and cdcai/TowerScout, so issue creation fails and the script (ci_publish_issue.py lines 179-227) falls back to PR comments / Actions summary / gists — and PR #46 already has 11 auto-posted 'Repository: J-Schulein/TowerScout / Workflow: Task-087...' CI-log comments (first 2026-07-06T22:18Z, latest 2026-07-07T22:03Z), so the spam is demonstrably live, just as PR-comment spam rather than issues. Material claim, severity, and recommendation all stand; if upstream ever enables issues it becomes issue spam exactly as claimed.

TASK-052' reason (tests/unit/test_azure_maps.py:13, test_framework.py:11, test_image_processing.py:14, test_validation.py:13), and ci.yml:98 runs `pytest tests/unit/` on every push/PR. ### 6.12 Completeness critic (pass 1; all four verified in pass 2)

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Completeness pass over the 11-dimension review, with independent spot-checks of angles the dimensions did not cover: .gitattributes/line endings, committed git config/hooks/submodules, Dockerfile base-image pinning, GitHub community-health files (SECURITY.md/dependabot/CODEOWNERS), wiki/Pages/Projects content, machine-specific paths, npm lockfile, and dependency-vulnerability posture. The review is impressively thorough — most of my candidate angles came back CLEAN: (1) .gitattributes exists and is sane ('* text=auto eol=lf', *.sh forced LF), so there is NO line-ending landmine for cdcai clones; only 7 upstream TowerScoutSite vendor files carry stale CRLF/mixed in the index (cosmetic renormalize noise, e.g. TowerScoutSite/assets/js/main.js i/crlf vs attr eol=lf). (2) No committed git hooks, no core.hooksPath, no .gitmodules, no git-LFS. (3) Zero tracked files reference Jonathan's machine or identity (git grep for 'Users.Jonat', 'C:/Users/Jonat', 'jonathan.schulein' → 0 hits repo-wide including .agent_work). (4) Both wikis are empty (github.com/{J-Schulein,cdcai}/TowerScout/wiki redirects to the repo root page), GitHub Pages disabled on both, no classic or v2 Projects content, Discussions disabled — nothing extra to migrate. (5) package-lock.json is tracked and Dockerfile uses 'npm ci', so the frontend build is lockfile-reproducible. (6) Compose image: refs and GHCR registry-org ownership are already adequately covered by packaging-2/migration-3 (ghcr.io/j-schulein hardcoded in 3 shipped files). (7) Open-issues content is a non-topic: has_issues=false on both repos (only 'open issue' on the fork is PR #46 itself). Four verified gaps remain, none release-blocking: no SECURITY.md/dependabot/CODEOWNERS anywhere (and no cdcai org-level .github fallback — API 404); the review never audited dependency-CVE posture (torch pinned at 2.2.1 while webapp/ts_en.py:101 relies on weights_only=True, the exact boundary CVE-2025-32434 bypasses in torch<2.6.0, and the CI Trivy scan is continue-on-error with SARIF results landing in a Security tab that does not migrate); Dockerfile base images are tag-pinned only with an EOL node:18 build stage (ci-4 flagged Node 18 only in the workflow, not the shipped image build chain); and the agent-work-2 'export backlog to GitHub issues' recommendation silently depends on someone enabling Issues, which are disabled on both fork and upstream.

#### `completeness-2` · MEDIUM · SELF-VERIFIED — Dependency-vulnerability posture never audited: torch pinned at 2.2.1 whose weights_only=True safety boundary (relied on in ts_en.py) is bypassed by CVE-2025-32434; CI security scan is purely advisory and its results do not migrate

- **Location:** webapp/requirements.txt:16, webapp/ts_en.py:99-107, .github/workflows/ci.yml:157-171
- **Evidence:** webapp/requirements.txt pins `torch==2.2.1` / `torchvision==0.17.1` (Feb-2024 releases; also baked into Dockerfile:52-55). webapp/ts_en.py:99-107 loads the EfficientNet checkpoint from the distributed 800MB asset bundle via `torch.load(str(path_best), weights_only=True, ...)` with a TypeError fallback to plain torch.load — weights_only=True is exactly the boundary CVE-2025-32434 (GHSA-53q9-r3pm-6pq6, RCE, fixed in torch 2.6.0) bypasses for torch<=2.5.1; the YOLOv5 vendor path (webapp/vendor/yolov5_local/models/experimental.py:98) uses ultralytics torch_load full-pickle loading, unsafe-by-design. ci.yml:157-164 runs trivy-action with format 'sarif' and `continue-on-error: true` (no severity/exit-code gate), and the SARIF upload (ci.yml:166-171, also continue-on-error) lands in the FORK's Security tab, which does not transfer on a git push. No review dimension (secrets covered credentials only; ci-5 covered advisory-ness generically) examined what known CVEs ship in the pinned dependency set of the stable release.
- **Recommendation:** Do NOT upgrade torch this week; instead (a) state the pinned-dependency CVE posture and the sha256-pinned-asset mitigation in the stable release notes / handoff doc, (b) add a torch>=2.6 upgrade task to the backlog export (alongside TASK-078), and (c) tell cdcai to enable Dependabot/code-scanning on their side since fork scan results do not migrate.
- **Migration relevance:** The stable v0.1.0 release will freeze torch 2.2.1 with a published critical CVE in the exact API the code relies on, and cdcai inherits zero visibility: the advisory Trivy results stay behind on the fork. Mitigating factor: exploitation requires a tampered asset bundle, and the bundle sha256 is pinned in the release manifest — so this is a documentation/backlog item, not a release blocker (a last-minute torch upgrade would invalidate the completed GPU/CPU parity validation).

#### `completeness-1` · LOW · SELF-VERIFIED — No SECURITY.md, dependabot config, or CODEOWNERS anywhere — and no cdcai org-level fallback

- **Location:** .github/ (fork, HEAD 533e18b) and https://api.github.com/repos/cdcai/.github
- **Evidence:** `git ls-files | grep -iE 'CODEOWNERS|SECURITY|dependabot|FUNDING'` matches only three .agent_work docs with 'security' in their names (TASK-001-api-key-security.md, decisions/003, decisions/012) — no SECURITY.md, no .github/dependabot.yml, no CODEOWNERS at any path. `ls .github/` = PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md, copilot-instructions.md, instructions, workflows only. `curl https://api.github.com/repos/cdcai/.github` → 404, so no org-default community files exist to inherit. The repo otherwise brands itself security-first (bandit + Trivy in ci.yml, security ADRs in .agent_work/decisions/).
- **Recommendation:** Before handoff add a minimal SECURITY.md (report-to address decided with cdcai) and .github/dependabot.yml (pip + npm + github-actions ecosystems); skip CODEOWNERS or leave it to cdcai since owner identities change at transfer.
- **Migration relevance:** A public-health-agency (CDC) repo with no vulnerability-reporting channel and no automated dependency updates is a professionalism gap the docs dimension (which audited LICENSE/NOTICE/CONTRIBUTING) never mentioned. CODEOWNERS is moot pre-transfer but SECURITY.md and dependabot.yml are 10-minute adds that materially help the new owner.

#### `completeness-3` · LOW · SELF-VERIFIED — Dockerfile base images are tag-pinned only, and the frontend build stage uses EOL node:18 — the shipped image build chain is not reproducible for cdcai

- **Location:** Dockerfile:3, Dockerfile:13
- **Evidence:** Dockerfile:3 `FROM node:18-bookworm-slim AS frontend` and Dockerfile:13 `FROM python:3.11-slim-bookworm AS runtime` — mutable tags, no @sha256 digests, plus unpinned `# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1` (Dockerfile:1). Node 18 reached end-of-life 2025-04-30, so the stage that builds the production JS bundle gets no security patches. The review's pinning findings (ci-4 'SHA-pinning convention', Node 18 EOL) covered only the GitHub Actions workflow, and packaging/migration covered only the built image's digest — no dimension looked at the Dockerfile FROM lines.
- **Recommendation:** Either pin both FROM lines by digest (and note the node 18→20/22 upgrade as a backlog item for cdcai), or at minimum record in the handoff notes that rebuilding the image uses floating base tags and an EOL Node major.

Not a blocker for this week's release (that image is already built and digest-pinned), but a first-rebuild surprise for the new owner.

### 6.13 Pass 2: critic-finding verification

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Adversarially re-verified all four pass-1 completeness-critic findings; all four CONFIRMED at their original severities (low/medium/low/info), with materially corrected evidence on completeness-2. (1) No SECURITY.md/dependabot/CODEOWNERS anywhere in tracked files; .github/ holds only a PR template, copilot instructions, and 3 workflows; both cdcai/.github and j-schulein/.github are HTTP 404. (2) torch==2.2.1 (requirements.txt:16) is inside CVE-2025-32434's affected range (<2.6.0, fixed 2.6.0, Critical CVSS 9.3; GHSA-53q9-r3pm-6pq6 explicitly states weights_only=True still yields RCE); ts_en.py:99-107 matches the claim (though its TypeError fallback is dead code under the pin), and the vendored YOLO path is worse than pass 1 said — ultralytics 8.3.249's torch_load (imported at experimental.py:9, called at :98) explicitly forces weights_only=False, so a torch upgrade alone will not harden it. The stated mitigation is real and stronger than claimed: per-asset sha256s in webapp/asset_manifest.v1.json are enforced file-by-file at setup staging (TowerScoutBootstrap.ps1:1388-1397) plus manifest-identity and ZIP-sidecar checks; runtime hash re-verification defaults off (compose.yaml:17). No user-supplied checkpoint path exists, so document-and-backlog (not release-blocker) is the right call. (3) Dockerfile:3/13 verified verbatim; Node 18 EOL 2025-04-30 confirmed — and Node 20 is also now EOL (2026-04-30), so the backlog bump should target node:22; the node stage is build-only and never ships in the runtime image. (4) has_issues=false confirmed live on both cdcai/TowerScout and J-Schulein/TowerScout, so the backlog-export runbook step depends on the cdcai owner enabling Issues. No finding was downgraded or refuted; no new criticals emerged from this area.

#### `p2-critic-2` · MEDIUM · SELF-VERIFIED — Verified completeness-2: torch 2.2.1 affected by CVE-2025-32434 (weights_only=True bypass); YOLO vendor path forces weights_only=False; sha256 setup enforcement is real

- **Location:** c:/Users/Jonat/Documents/TowerScout/TowerScout/webapp/requirements.txt:16; webapp/ts_en.py:99-107; webapp/vendor/yolov5_local/models/experimental.py:9,98
- **Evidence:** CONFIRMED with two corrections that strengthen it. Code claims verified: requirements.txt:16 pins torch==2.2.1 (torchvision==0.17.1 at :17, ultralytics==8.3.249 at :19); ts_en.py:101-105 calls torch.load(..., weights_only=True) with a TypeError fallback to plain torch.load at :106-107 (correction 1: the fallback is dead code under the 2.2.1 pin — torch>=1.13 accepts weights_only, so it never fires); experimental.py:98 calls torch_load imported at :9 from ultralytics.utils.patches, and in the pinned ultralytics v8.3.249 that wrapper explicitly sets kwargs['weights_only']=False for torch>=1.13 (verified from the v8.3.249 tag source on GitHub) — correction 2: even upgrading torch to >=2.6 would NOT harden the YOLO path because weights_only=False is forced, which is inherent to YOLOv5 full-object checkpoints. CVE facts verified via https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-53q9-r3pm-6pq6: it is CVE-2025-32434, Critical CVSS 9.3, affects pip torch <2.6.0 (so 2.2.1 is in range; pass-1's '<=2.5.1' is equivalent), fixed in 2.6.0, and the advisory explicitly states 'When loading model using torch.load with weights_only=True, it can still achieve RCE.' Mitigation verified STRONGER than pass 1 claimed: webapp/asset_manifest.v1.json pins per-asset sha256 (yolo newest.pt = 27315E15..., EfficientNet b5 = 645113BA...), and the setup flow enforces it per file — TowerScoutBootstrap.ps1:1388-1397 hashes every staged asset against the manifest and throws on mismatch, plus :1080-1084 requires the ZIP's embedded manifest to byte-match the control package's webapp/asset_manifest.v1.json, plus the ZIP-level .sha256 sidecar check at :694-707. Runtime re-verification exists (webapp/ts_assets.py:164-169) but defaults OFF (compose.yaml:17 TOWERSCOUT_VERIFY_ASSET_HASHES:-0). Exploitability: grep shows the only torch.load/attempt_load call sites load the fixed asset-bundle paths (get_en_model_dir()/b5_unweighted_best.pt, model_params/yolov5/newest.pt); no user-supplied checkpoint path exists in the webapp.
- **Recommendation:** Keep severity medium and the document-and-backlog disposition — verified NOT a release blocker: exploitation requires a tampered checkpoint on disk, which the setup-time per-file sha256 enforcement prevents for the documented install path, and there is no network-reachable model-load surface. Backlog items to record: (a) upgrade torch/torchvision to >=2.6.0 pair after release (needs model-parity re-validation, so wrong to do before 07-10); (b) note that the YOLO path needs an ultralytics/vendor change, not just a torch bump, because torch_load forces weights_only=False; (c) optionally set TOWERSCOUT_VERIFY_ASSET_HASHES=1 as a cheap defense-in-depth default. Document the trust model (weights are trusted, integrity-pinned inputs) in the release notes/security section.
- **Migration relevance:** Must appear in the migration handover as a known-accepted risk with the backlog items above, so the cdcai owner inherits the rationale; Dependabot (p2-critic-1) would have flagged this class automatically.

#### `p2-github-1` · MEDIUM · CONFIRMED — Local-only tag uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 and local-only branch feature/podman-gpu-cdi-enablement exist only in the working copy; 'git push --tags' during the v0.1.0 release cut would publish the stray UAT tag

- **Location:** c:/Users/Jonat/Documents/TowerScout/TowerScout (.git refs: refs/tags/uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 -> 64c2b5dd, refs/heads/feature/podman-gpu-cdi-enablement -> d0804bb)
- **Evidence:** 'git tag' lists 15 local tags but 'git ls-remote --tags origin' lists only 14 — uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 (64c2b5dd, 2026-06-15 'fix(task-083): Podman 5.8.2 provider guardrail + GPU VMType gate (UAT PR #33)') is absent from origin, and 'git branch -r --contains 64c2b5dd' is empty (commit unreachable from every remote ref). Likewise 'git branch -vv' shows feature/podman-gpu-cdi-enablement (d0804bb 'WIP: snapshot local podman/GPU implementation before switching to PR #33') with no upstream, and d0804bb is not an ancestor of any refs/remotes/* ref. All other local tags match origin exactly.
- **Recommendation:** Before cutting v0.1.0: push the new tag explicitly ('git push origin v0.1.0') and never 'git push --tags' from this working copy, or the stray UAT tag and its unpublished commit land in the public tag namespace right at release time. Decide disposition of both local-only refs — they are superseded by merged PR #33 work, so either delete them locally ('git tag -d uat-local-fix-2026-06-15'; 'git branch -D feature/podman-gpu-cdi-enablement') or archive them to a bundle in personal storage first. Note they will silently NOT transfer to cdcai in any GitHub-side migration.
