- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced fully: 15 local tags vs 14 on origin ls-remote; uat-local-fix-2026-06-15 -> 64c2b5dd with exact commit message/date as stated; 'git branch -r --contains 64c2b5dd' empty; feature/podman-gpu-cdi-enablement -> d0804bb with no upstream in 'git branch -vv' and no remote branch containing it. Per-SHA diff confirms all other 14 local tags match origin exactly. One strengthening detail: 64c2b5dd is contained by NO branch at all (local or remote) — only the stray tag itself makes it reachable, so 'git tag -d' orphans the commit; archive to a bundle first if retention is wanted. push --tags hazard is standard git behavior. Medium severity stands.

### 6.15 Pass 2: User Guidance alignment (out-of-repo)

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Audited all out-of-repo user guidance against the decided release path (stable v0.1.0 from main post-PR#46, guided TLS repair, eventual cdcai migration). ### 6.16 Pass 2: migration-runbook dry run

> **Reviewer's dimension summary:** Runbook dry-run completed end-to-end in a scratch clone (C:/Users/Jonat/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/.../scratchpad/ts-dryrun). The core plan is sound: git merge cdcai/main conflicts ONLY in README.md (2 hunks, resolution validated to a clean merge commit 418417f), the push to cdcai is a confirmed fast-forward (~30 MiB pack), release-cut and packaging commands verified against source, asset-ZIP rename target name and sidecar format confirmed byte-level, and gh release syntax is straightforward. Three real surprises the Developer MUST plan around: (1) HIGH — the naive image copy 'docker pull digest, tag, push' does NOT preserve digests because published images are OCI image indexes with buildx provenance (verified Content-Type on GHCR); crane/regctl/skopeo are not installed, so either download regctl/crane first or re-dispatch container-publish.yml inside cdcai and repackage with the new digest. (2) MEDIUM — nobody on this machine can push to cdcai today: git push --dry-run returned 403 'Permission to cdcai/TowerScout.git denied to Jschulein' and gh CLI is entirely unauthenticated; write access to cdcai/TowerScout and ghcr.io/cdcai is a critical-path dependency before 07-10. (3) MEDIUM — a fresh Windows clone of the repo FAILED checkout ('Filename too long', longest tracked path 156 chars under .agent_work/) in any directory deeper than ~104 chars; migration docs and the Developer's own steps need core.longpaths=true. Plus a sequencing dependency: 8 user-facing docs staged into every release ZIP hardcode github.com/J-Schulein/TowerScout/releases and must be edited BEFORE tagging v0.1.0 if the stable release is to live on cdcai. Positive checks: PR #46 does not touch README.md so merge order vs PR #46 is irrelevant; no GitHub release is attached to the stale v0.1.0 tag, so tag delete/recreate is safe; sidecar hash comparison is case-insensitive so the WinPS Get-FileHash uppercase gotcha cannot break setup (still write lowercase for consistency); the 800,655,295-byte assets ZIP is within GitHub's 2 GiB asset limit.

#### `p2-runbook-4` · HIGH · CONFIRMED — Image copy to ghcr.io/cdcai: 'docker pull digest -> tag -> push' does NOT preserve the pinned digest (images are OCI indexes with buildx provenance); crane/regctl/skopeo are not installed

- **Location:** ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout manifests (v0.1.0-rc7.1-cpu = sha256:14b6ef523f93..., v0.1.0-rc7.1-cuda121 = sha256:95f1f3967294...); .github/workflows/container-publish.yml:82-93 (buildx --push without --provenance=false); this machine's PATH
- **Evidence:** Anonymous GHCR API check returned Content-Type: application/vnd.oci.image.index.v1+json for both rc7.1 tags — i.e., the digest recorded in IMAGE.txt/release manifests is an OCI IMAGE INDEX digest (buildx default provenance attestation). 'docker pull ...@<index-digest>' resolves the inner amd64 manifest; a subsequent 'docker push' to ghcr.io/cdcai re-creates a single image manifest with a DIFFERENT digest and drops the index/attestation, so the digest baked into the release package would 404 under ghcr.io/cdcai. Tooling inventory on this machine: docker 29.6.1 (Docker Desktop RUNNING), podman present, gh 2.92.0 present but UNAUTHENTICATED, crane NOT FOUND, regctl NOT FOUND, skopeo NOT FOUND; winget and choco available, scoop/go absent.
- **Recommendation:** Option ranking: (1) BEST — download the single-binary regctl (no admin, no package manager): curl -L -o regctl.exe https://github.com/regclient/regclient/releases/latest/download/regctl-windows-amd64.exe ; regctl registry login ghcr.io -u <cdcai-member> (PAT with write:packages) ; regctl image copy ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout@sha256:<v0.1.0-cpu-digest> ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout:v0.1.0-cpu (repeat for cuda121). This copies the index byte-for-byte and preserves the digest, keeping the rc7.1-validated bytes and package IMAGE.txt valid. ('choco install crane' + 'crane cp <src> <dst>' is equivalent.) (2) Re-dispatch container-publish.yml in cdcai/TowerScout after the merged main is pushed — the workflow derives the image name from GITHUB_REPOSITORY so it publishes ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout automatically with the org's own GITHUB_TOKEN; BUT this rebuilds (new digests, non-reproducible), so packages must be regenerated with the new digests and validation evidence re-linked; also requires cdcai org Actions enabled and workflow packages:write permitted. (3) LAST RESORT docker pull/tag/push — accept a new digest, capture it from 'docker push' output, repackage, and verify with 'docker buildx imagetools inspect ghcr.io/cdcai/towerscout:v0.1.0-cpu'. In all cases 'docker login ghcr.io -u <username>' needs a classic PAT with write:packages (+read:packages) held by someone with cdcai package write access — the Jschulein account is 403 on cdcai (see p2-runbook-5). First push creates the GHCR package PRIVATE; a cdcai admin must flip visibility to public and link it to the repo or user setup pulls will fail.
- **Migration relevance:** If the runbook assumed docker pull/push preserves the digest, every downstream artifact (IMAGE.txt, release-manifest.v1.json, setup preflight digest pinning) would silently point at a nonexistent digest on cdcai; this is the single most likely day-of failure.
- **Verifier's note:** Reproduced GHCR check anonymously (token endpoint + manifest HEAD): both v0.1.0-rc7.1-cpu and -cuda121 return Content-Type application/vnd.oci.image.index.v1+json with digests sha256:14b6ef523f93... and sha256:95f1f3967294... exactly as stated; index body contains an amd64 manifest plus a vnd.docker.reference.type=attestation-manifest entry (buildx provenance), and container-publish.yml lines 82-91 use buildx --push with no --provenance=false. rc7.1-testing/cpu/IMAGE.txt pins that same INDEX digest, confirming the failure premise. Tooling inventory reproduced: crane/regctl/skopeo NOT FOUND, docker 29.6.1, podman 5.8.2, gh 2.92.0 unauthenticated, winget+choco present, scoop/go absent. regctl-windows-amd64.exe confirmed present in regclient's latest release (v0.11.5), so the literal download command works. One nuance not changing the conclusion: on Docker 29's containerd store a re-push may emit a pruned index rather than a bare manifest, but the digest still differs because the attestation manifest is never pulled — the @digest pin still 404s under ghcr.io/cdcai. High severity appropriate.

#### `p2-runbook-6` · LOW · CONFIRMED — gh release create on cdcai: exact syntax validated; gh 2.92.0 installed but not logged in; 800 MB asset within limits, expect minutes-long upload

- **Location:** gh CLI on this machine (gh auth status: 'You are not logged into any GitHub hosts'); asset set verified from release v0.1.0-rc7.1 (6 assets incl. 800,655,295-byte ZIP)
- **Evidence:** gh --version = 2.92.0 (2026-04-28); gh auth status exits 1, unauthenticated. The rc7.1 release's 6-asset shape confirmed via API: cpu.zip (141,786 B), cpu.zip.sha256, cuda121.zip (141,811 B), cuda121.zip.sha256, assets ZIP (800,655,295 B), assets ZIP .sha256 (134 B). GitHub's per-asset limit is 2 GiB, so the 800 MB ZIP fits. Without --verify-tag, gh silently creates the tag at the default-branch tip if it does not exist on the remote — a footgun if the tag push was skipped.
- **Recommendation:** Prerequisite: gh auth login (choose the account holding cdcai write; browser flow). Then, from the folder holding the artifacts: gh release create v0.1.0 --repo cdcai/TowerScout --title "TowerScout v0.1.0" --notes-file release-notes-v0.1.0.md --latest --verify-tag dist\towerscout-v0.1.0-cpu.zip dist\towerscout-v0.1.0-cpu.zip.sha256 dist\towerscout-v0.1.0-cuda121.zip dist\towerscout-v0.1.0-cuda121.zip.sha256 release-v0.1.0\towerscout-v0.1.0-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip release-v0.1.0\towerscout-v0.1.0-assets-towerscout-v1-assets-2026-05-05.zip.sha256 . Keep --verify-tag (fails fast if the v0.1.0 tag was not pushed to cdcai first) and --latest (explicit; deterministic even though the first non-prerelease would be auto-marked). Upload expectation for 6.4 Gbit: ~2 min at 50 Mbps up, ~11 min at 10 Mbps — do not interrupt; if a single asset fails mid-transfer, re-send just that file with: gh release upload v0.1.0 <file> --repo cdcai/TowerScout --clobber.
- **Migration relevance:** Final publish step of the runbook; only auth and tag-push ordering can break it.
- **Verifier's note:** gh 2.92.0 (2026-04-28) confirmed; 'gh auth status' = not logged into any hosts. rc7.1 6-asset shape confirmed via API with byte-exact sizes: cpu.zip 141,786; cpu.zip.sha256 99; cuda121.zip 141,811; cuda121.zip.sha256 103; assets ZIP 800,655,295; assets sidecar 134 — under GitHub's 2 GiB per-asset limit. All gh flags in the recommendation (--repo, --notes-file, --latest, --verify-tag, release upload --clobber) are valid gh 2.x syntax, and the without---verify-tag tag-autocreation footgun is documented gh behavior. Upload-time math checks out (6.4 Gbit ≈ 2 min at 50 Mbps).

- **Evidence:** Rate limiter keys are still bare client_ip for geocode (30/600 s), maps proxy, and uploads (10/60 s) — only config-* routes are scoped; the harness works around it with a hardcoded 65 s drain plus 8 s/tile spacing. Any browser tab or curl activity against the same cell during a harness run shares the same IP budget and can inject spurious 429s ('Rate limit exceeded for image uploads'), corrupting a parity pass.
- **Recommendation:** Protocol rules: (R1) do not touch the app UI or issue API calls while a harness pass is running; (R2) keep S3/S4 manual API calls ≥60 s clear of harness start; (R3) if a 429 appears in any fixture_tile_*.json, discard that pass and re-run it after 60 s rather than judging parity from a rate-limited run — a 429-poisoned pass shows count drops that mimic a real parity failure (abort-gate G4 false positive).
- **Migration relevance:** This latent finding survives into v0.1.0; the migration handover notes should carry it so cdcai-side scripted validation doesn't misdiagnose 429s as detection regressions.
- **Verifier's note:** Verified at 533e18b (current checkout): rate_limiter.is_allowed keyed on bare client_ip at towerscout.py:2191 and :2241 (both 30 req/600 s geocode routes — finding's cited ranges 2190-2192/2240-2242 correct), plus bare-key 10/60 s at :2753 and :3407 (uploads/maps) and 30/60 at :863 and :2616; ONLY config routes are scoped (config-validate:{ip} at :2020, config-save:{ip} at :2035). Harness workaround lines confirmed (comment+65 s sleep at 97-100, 8 s spacing at 110). QA doc finding 1 independently marks this 'still present at PR-46 head 533e18b' with the 12-GET-then-POST repro. Rules R1-R3 correctly prevent a 429-poisoned pass from mimicking a G4 parity failure ('Rate limit exceeded for image uploads' is the documented symptom).

#### `p2-claims-7` · LOW · SELF-VERIFIED — Nothing hardcodes release version / cutting stable needs zero source edits — NEEDS-NUANCE

- **Location:** 533e18b:scripts/package-release.ps1:1-41; 533e18b:.github/workflows/container-publish.yml:5-7,46; 533e18b:compose.yaml:3; 533e18b:scripts/lib/TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117
- **Evidence:** The version half is fully correct: package-release.ps1 takes -Version as a parameter (default 'local', line 2) and -Image/-ImageDigest with TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE/TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE_DIGEST env fallbacks (lines 33-41); container-publish.yml takes the tag as a workflow_dispatch input (lines 5-7) and derives the image repo from ${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,} (line 46), so it auto-adapts to whichever repo runs it. Repo-wide grep for version strings at 533e18b hits only test fixtures, docs, a workflow description, and TowerScoutHostHelper.ps1:3's script-internal '0.1.0-gate1' constant — none gate a release cut. HOWEVER, the fallback image NAMESPACE is hardcoded in three source defaults — compose.yaml:3 and TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117 ('ghcr.io/j-schulein/towerscout:latest-cpu', reached only when TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE/.env are unset) and package-release.ps1:37 — plus test assertions. Cutting stable ON THE FORK truly needs zero source edits, but the memo's own cdcai migration plan requires editing exactly these lines; if that namespace pass must land before the stable packages are built (so v0.1.0 artifacts don't bake in j-schulein registry fallbacks), then the release effort as a whole does involve source edits.
- **Recommendation:** Rephrase the memo claim as: 'No source edit is needed to set the version — it is fully parameterized. The ghcr.io/j-schulein namespace defaults (compose.yaml:3, TowerScoutPodmanGpu.ps1:117, package-release.ps1:37 + test assertions) are a separate deliberate edit set; decide explicitly whether the namespace pass lands before or after the v0.1.0 cut, because packages built before it will carry j-schulein registry fallbacks into the cdcai era.' Note runtime override exists (TOWERSCOUT_IMAGE env / .env file), so post-cut mitigation is possible but ugly.
- **Migration relevance:** Directly affects sequencing of the 07-10 stable cut vs the cdcai namespace pass; ambiguity here could force a v0.1.0 re-cut or re-tag during migration.

- **Location:** c:/Users/Jonat/Documents/TowerScout/TowerScout — v0.1.0-rc7.1:webapp/ts_geocoding.py:480-489 vs 533e18b:webapp/ts_geocoding.py:175-221,509-560

- **Recommendation:** No change needed; the memo statement is accurate including the ~60s TTL detail.
